In the name of God, Most Gracious, Most Merciful. O God, to You belongs praise.

Abū Saʿīd al-Ḥasan ibn al-Ḥusayn al-Sukkarī said: Abū Jaʿfar Muḥammad ibn Ḥabīb related to us. He said: Abū Ḥātim al-Bajalī mentioned, on the authority of Hishām ibn Muḥammad, from his father, from Abū Ṣāliḥ, from Ibn ʿAbbās ﵁ who said: From Adam ﵇ to Noah were two thousand two hundred years. From Noah to Abraham ﵇ were one thousand one hundred and forty-three years; it is also said, one thousand one hundred and forty-two years. From Abraham to Moses were five hundred and seventy-five years; it is also said, [five hundred and] sixty-five years. From Moses to David were five hundred and ninety years; it is also said, [five hundred and] seventy-nine years. From David to Jesus were one thousand and fifty-three years. From Jesus to Muḥammad, may God bless him and all the prophets, were six hundred years. This is the account of Ibn al-Kalbī.

Abū Ḥātim al-Bajalī also mentioned, on the authority of al-Haytham ibn ʿAdī, from one of the People of the Book, who said: From the time of Adam ﵇ to the Flood were two thousand two hundred and fifty-six years. From the Flood to Abraham’s death were one thousand and twenty years. From Abraham’s death to the entry of the Children of Israel into Egypt were seventy-five years. From Jacob’s entry into Egypt to Moses’ departure from Egypt were one hundred and fifty years. From Moses’ departure from Egypt to the building of the Temple were two hundred and sixty years. From the building of the Temple to the reign of Bukhtanaṣṣar and the destruction of the Temple were two thousand two hundred and forty years.

This is the account of al-Haytham ibn ʿAdī. It is also said—and some have deemed this correct—that it was one thousand two hundred and forty-five years. From the Prophet’s mission ﷺ to the year 245 were two hundred and fifty-eight years.

The Lifespans of the Prophets, may God bless them

Muḥammad ibn Ḥabīb said: A Jew at Nahr Nāthān told me: Adam ﷺ lived nine hundred and thirty years. Seth lived seven hundred and twelve years. Al-Kalbī said: Rather, he lived nine hundred and thirty years. Enosh lived nine hundred and five years. Ibn al-Kalbī said: Fifty-seven years.

Kenan lived nine hundred and ten years. Ibn al-Kalbī said: Nine hundred and twenty years. Mahalalel lived eight hundred and ninety-five years. Jared lived nine hundred and sixty-two years. Enoch, who is Idrīs, was the first of Adam’s descendants to be given prophethood ﵇; in his time idols were worshipped. He lived three hundred and five years. Ibn al-Kalbī said: He lived until God, mighty and exalted is He, raised him, at the age of one hundred and sixty-five years. Methuselah, son of Enoch, lived nine hundred and sixty-nine years. Ibn al-Kalbī said: One thousand one hundred and seventy years. Lamech lived seven hundred and seventy-seven years. Noah lived nine hundred and fifty years.

Revelation came to him when he was four hundred and eighty years old. He then called his people to his prophethood, but only a few believed. Then God commanded him to make the Ark, so he made it and boarded it when he was [six hundred years old]. Those who were drowned were drowned. After that, he lived for three hundred and fifty years. It is said that he lived for five hundred and ninety-eight years. Ibn al-Kalbī said: Nine hundred years. Arpachshad lived four hundred and ninety-eight years. Shelah lived four hundred and thirty-three years.

Al-Kalbī said: Four hundred and ninety-three years. Eber lived one hundred and thirty-four years. Al-Kalbī said: Four hundred and sixty-three years. Peleg lived two hundred and thirty-nine years. Al-Kalbī said: Two hundred and ninety years.

Reu lived two hundred and thirty-two years. Al-Kalbī said: Two hundred and sixty-nine years. Serug lived two hundred and thirty years. Nahor lived one hundred and forty-eight years. Terah, who is Āzar, lived two hundred and fifty years. Abraham ﵇ lived one hundred and seventy-five years; it is also said, one hundred and ninety-five years. Isaac lived one hundred and fifty years; it is also said, one hundred and eighty-five years. Joseph lived one hundred and twenty years. Moses, son of Amram son of Qahath, son of Levi, son of Jacob, lived one hundred and twenty years. Aaron, son of Amram, lived one hundred and twenty-three years. Job, son of Zāriḥ, son of Amūṣ, son of Līfazar, son of Isaac, son of Abraham, lived two hundred years. David, son of Jesse, son of Obed, son of Salmūn, son of Bāʿaz, son of Nahshon, son of ʿAmmī Nādhīb, son of Rām, son of Ḥaḍrūn, son of Perez, son of Judah, son of Jacob, son of Isaac, son of Abraham, lived seventy years. There is a great discrepancy among these accounts, and between the first account and that of Ibn al-Kalbī. God knows best how it was and which account is more correct, for I have not found which of them is the most correct.

Abū Saʿīd said: Muḥammad ibn Ḥabīb told me: Someone I had met mentioned to me: The chronology by which the Arabs reckoned began with the Year of the Dispersion. The reason for the Dispersion was that Bukhtanaṣṣar ordered an attack on the people of Ḥuḍūr and the people of Bāʿarbāyā, whose doorways had no gates. He marched toward them and put the Arabs to the sword as he advanced, until he reached Ḥuḍūr. As for who ordered Bukhtanaṣṣar to attack and kill those people, according to what has been related—and God knows best—it was God ﵎ who revealed to Ibrākhiyā, son of Aḥniyā, son of Zarbāyil, son of Shāthīl, a descendant of Judah, son of Jacob, commanding him to order Bukhtanaṣṣar to attack those we have mentioned. He marched until he reached the land of Yemen, to a place there called Ḥuḍūr. It was inhabited by the sons of Ishmael, son of Abraham: Qudmān, Raʿawīl, and Yaʾman. They were the People of the Ditch, who had killed their prophet Ḥanẓalah ibn Ṣafwān. Bukhtanaṣṣar surprised them while they were unaware and began killing them. They fled. It was about them, God knows best, that the following was revealed: “When they sensed Our might, behold, they were fleeing from it” (21:12), through His mighty words: “Their cry did not cease until We made them like harvested stubble, silent” (21:15). So Bukhtanaṣṣar’s swords cut them down.

God, mighty and exalted is He—and He knows best—had also commanded Jeremiah, son of Hilkiah, who was the prophet of the Children of Israel at that time, to go to Mecca and bring out Maʿadd ibn ʿAdnān, a descendant of whom was Muḥammad ﷺ who would be sent at the end of time. Jeremiah set out and brought him out while he was a young man, then took him to Syria. When Bukhtanaṣṣar withdrew from the Arabs, Jeremiah returned him to Mecca, while the land of the Arabs lay empty. Maʿadd had children there. When they became numerous, they divided Tihāmah into seven portions, one portion for each group. When they grew more numerous, space became tight and they competed with one another. Wars broke out among them, and they dispersed in search of pasture and room to expand. They moved out from Tihāmah to the highlands. There are lengthy accounts of their dispersion and the places where they settled. The Arabs’ chronology thus began with the Year of the Dispersion and the departure of Maʿadd’s descendants from Mecca.

Then they reckoned from the Year of Treachery. The reason for the Year of Treachery was that Aws and Ḥaṣbah, sons of Aznam ibn ʿUbayd ibn Thaʿlabah ibn Yarbuʿ ibn Ḥanẓalah ibn Zayd Manāt ibn Tamīm, set out on pilgrimage. At the boundary markers of the Ḥaram they encountered a king who had the covering of the Kaʿbah with him.

They killed him, took what he had with him, and entered Mecca. When the days of Minā came and the people had quieted down, the news reached them. The Arabs then betrayed the Banū Tamīm and some of them plundered one another. This was therefore called the “Year of Treachery.”

That was Quraysh’s chronological reckoning up to the Year of the Elephant, on Sunday, with thirteen nights remaining in Muḥarram. The first of Muḥarram was Friday, forty years before the Prophet’s mission ﷺ. His mission began one hundred and fifty years after the Year of Treachery, in the twentieth year of the reign of Kisrā Abrāwīz; it is also said that sixteen years of his reign had passed. At that time Bādhām, Abū Mihrān, ruled Yemen. Iyās ibn Qubayṣah al-Ṭāʾī ruled al-Ḥīrah, with the Persian al-Nakhīr Jān; their rule had then lasted two years and four months.

As for the Bedouin Arabs, they reckoned time only by events in particular years, such as a war or a calamity, and the like. Among such references is the saying of al-Nābighah al-Jaʿdī:

Whoever asks about me, I amone of the young men in the days of al-Khunān.

Among them is the saying of al-ʿUjair al-Salūlī:

She saw me stooped over this morning; whoever wasa young man before the Year of the Water is old.

And the saying of one of them:

I complained to ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz of hardship,from the white plague or the time of al-Qatād.

Such references occur frequently in their poetry.

The Messenger of God ﷺ was born on Monday, when two nights of the month of Rabīʿ al-Awwal had passed or, it is said, when eight nights of the month of Rabīʿ al-Awwal had passed. His father, ʿAbd Allāh, died when he was twenty-eight months old. His mother died when he was eight years old. She was Āminah, daughter of Wahb ibn ʿAbd Manāf ibn Zuhrah ibn Kilāb. Her mother was Barrah, daughter of ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd al-Dār ibn Quṣayy. Her mother was Umm Ḥabīb, daughter of Asad ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn Quṣayy. Her mother was Barrah, daughter of ʿAwf ibn ʿUbayd ibn ʿUwayj ibn ʿAdī ibn Kaʿb ibn Luʾayy ibn Ghālib. Her mother was Qulābah, daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn Mālik ibn Ḥubāshah ibn ʿĀdiyah ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿah ibn Kaʿb ibn Ṭābikhah ibn Liḥyān ibn Hudhayl ibn Mudrikah ibn Ilyās ibn Muḍar. Her mother was Umaymah, daughter of Mālik ibn Ghanm ibn Liḥyān ibn Ghādiyah ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿah. Her mother was a daughter of Kahf al-Ẓulm ibn Yarbuʿ ibn Nāṣirah ibn Ghāḍirah ibn Ḥuṭayṭ ibn Jusham ibn Qusī, who was Thaqīf ibn Munabbih.

The Prophet ﷺ was in the care of his uncle Abū Ṭālib. Abū Ṭālib took him to Buṣrā in Syria when he was nine years old. The Prophet ﷺ placed the Black Stone in its position when Quraysh disputed over where to place it; he was then thirty-five years and two months old. He married Khadījah ﵂ daughter of Khuwaylid ibn Asad when he was twenty-five years old. She was forty years old. Then Gabriel ﵇ came to him on Saturday night and Sunday night. On Monday, he appeared to him with God’s message, mighty and exalted is He. He taught him ablution and prayer, and taught him: “Recite in the name of your Lord who created” (96:1). On the Monday when revelation came to him, the Prophet ﷺ was forty years old. Khadījah, may God’s good pleasure be upon her, accepted Islam with him. The stars were pelted after his mission began, twenty days later. He remained in Mecca for thirteen years, then migrated to Medina in the month of Rabīʿ al-Awwal. The Battle of Badr then took place in the month of Ramaḍān. There were eighteen months between his migration ﷺ and the Battle of Badr. Then came Uḥud, in Shawwāl, one year after Badr. The Trench took place in Shawwāl, one year after Uḥud. The Elephant came to Mecca in pre-Islamic times, and its companions perished on Sunday, with thirteen nights remaining in Muḥarram. The first of Muḥarram that year was Friday. The Prophet was born fifty days after the Elephant. He was born on a Monday. His wet nurse Ḥalīmah returned him to his mother when he was five years and two days old. ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib died when the Prophet ﷺ was eight years, two months, and ten days old. God, mighty and exalted is He, sent him to the people when he was forty years, two months, and ten days old. The stars were pelted twenty days after his mission began.

He was besieged in the pass five years, eleven months, and twenty-one days after that. They remained under siege for three years. Abū Ṭālib died six months after that. Khadīja ﵂ died three days after Abū Ṭālib, when forty-nine years, eight months, and twenty-four days had elapsed since the birth of the Messenger of God ﷺ. He set out for al-Ṭāʾif three months and eight days after that. He remained at al-Ṭāʾif until he returned to Mecca, where he entered under the protection of Muṭʿim ibn ʿAdī ibn Nawfal for a month and two days. The jinn of Naṣībīn came to him ﷺ and accepted Islam three months later. It was a year, six months, and six days until he was taken on the Night Journey ﷺ. It was a year, two months, and one day until the beginning of the year in which he migrated. That makes fifty-three years. Then the Messenger of God ﷺ migrated and remained [in Medina] for ten years.

Names of those who led the pilgrimage and names of the caliphs

In the eighth year after the Hijra, ʿAttāb ibn Asīd ibn Abī al-ʿĪṣ ibn Umayya led the pilgrimage. The Muslims stood with ʿAttāb, while the rest of the people remained polytheists and stood at the pilgrimage stations where they had stood in the pre-Islamic period.

In the ninth year, Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq ﵀ led the pilgrimage, while the polytheists performed the pilgrimage at their stations in the pre-Islamic period. The Messenger of God ﷺ performed the pilgrimage in the tenth year.

He called it the Farewell Pilgrimage, and it was the Pilgrimage of Islam. There were no polytheists in it. Then he ﷺ died on Monday, two nights having passed in the month of Rabīʿ al-Awwal, at the age of sixty-three. Ibn al-Kalbī related, from his father, from Abū Ṣāliḥ, from Ibn ʿAbbās ﵁ that he said: “He was sixty-two and a half years old.”

Then Abū Bakr, whose name was ʿAtīq,

son of ʿUthmān ibn ʿĀmir ibn ʿAmr ibn Kaʿb ibn Saʿd ibn Taym ibn Murra ibn Kaʿb ibn Luʾayy ibn Ghālib, was appointed caliph. His mother was Umm al-Khayr, namely Salmā daughter of Ṣakhr ibn ʿĀmir ibn ʿAmr ibn Kaʿb ibn Saʿd ibn Taym ibn Murra. Her mother was Umayma, namely Dulāf daughter of ʿUbayd ibn al-Nāfidh ibn Murra ibn ʿAmr ibn Saʿd ibn Taym ibn Murra. Her mother was ʿĀtika daughter of ʿAbd Yālīl ibn Ghayra ibn Saʿd ibn Layth.

He assumed office in the month of Rabīʿ al-Awwal of the year eleven after the Prophet’s migration to Medina, and ruled for two years and four months, less eight days.

In the year eleven, ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb ﵀ led the pilgrimage; it is also said that it was ʿAttāb ibn Asīd, but the account naming ʿUmar is more firmly established. In the year twelve, he led the pilgrimage for the people— Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq ﵀. Some people said that Abū Bakr did not perform the pilgrimage during his caliphate. He ﵀ died in Jumādā al-Ākhira, with two nights remaining in the month. ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb led the funeral prayer for him.

Then ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb was appointed caliph,

son of Nufayl ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn Riyāḥ ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qurṭ ibn Razāḥ ibn ʿAdī ibn Kaʿb ibn Luʾayy ibn Ghālib. His mother was Ḥantama daughter of Hāshim ibn al-Mughīra ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn Makhzūm ibn Yaqaẓa ibn Murra. Her mother was al-Shifāʾ daughter of ʿAbd Qays ibn ʿAdī ibn Saʿd ibn Sahm ibn ʿAmr ibn Huṣayṣ ibn Kaʿb. Her mother was Āmina daughter of ʿAqīl ibn Kilāb ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Ḍarība ibn al-Ḥazmar ibn Salūl, of Khuzāʿa. Her mother was Umayma daughter of Ghubshān ibn ʿAbd ʿAmr ibn ʿAmr ibn Buʾayy ibn Malkān ibn Afṣā, of Khuzāʿa. Her mother was Umayma daughter of Jadʿān ibn ʿAmr ibn Kaʿb ibn Saʿd ibn Taym ibn Murra. Her mother was Khālida daughter of ʿAbd Rabbih ibn al-Nāfidh ibn Murra ibn Taym ibn Saʿd ibn Kaʿb, of Khuzāʿa. It is also said that the mother of Umayma daughter of Ghubshān was Umayma daughter of ʿAmr ibn Kaʿb ibn Saʿd ibn Taym ibn Murra; this is the more firmly established account.

He ruled for ten years and eight months. In the year thirteen, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf ibn ʿAbd ʿAwf ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Zuhra ibn— Kilāb led the pilgrimage for the people. In the year fourteen, ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb led the pilgrimage for the people. In the year fifteen, ʿUmar also led the pilgrimage. In that year were the battle of Yarmūk in Syria and the battle of al-Qādisiyya in Iraq. He likewise led the pilgrimage in the year sixteen, and in the year seventeen, the Year of the Ashes. He also led the pilgrimage in the year eighteen. In that year, the plague of ʿAmwās occurred in Syria, in the land of Palestine. He also led the pilgrimage in the year nineteen, in the year twenty, and in the year twenty-one, in which the battle of Nahāwand took place; and in the year twenty-two and the year twenty-three. In all these years, ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb led the pilgrimage for the people. He ﵀ was killed when three or four nights remained in Dhū al-Ḥijja of the year twenty-three, at the age of sixty-three. The shūrā continued for the remainder of Dhū al-Ḥijja. Ṣuhayb ibn Sinān al-Namrī led the funeral prayer for him. Abū Luʾluʾa the Magian killed him.

Then ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān was appointed caliph,

son of Abū al-ʿĀṣ ibn Umayya ibn ʿAbd Shams ibn ʿAbd Manāf ibn Quṣayy. His mother was Arwā daughter of Kurayz ibn Rabīʿa ibn Ḥabīb ibn ʿAbd Shams ibn ʿAbd Manāf.

Her mother was al-Bayḍāʾ, namely Umm Ḥakīm daughter of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. Her mother was Fāṭima daughter of ʿAmr ibn ʿĀʾidh ibn ʿImrān ibn Makhzūm ibn Yaqaẓa. Her mother was Ṣakhra daughter of ʿAbd ibn ʿImrān ibn Makhzūm ibn Yaqaẓa. Her mother was Takhmur daughter of ʿAbd ibn Quṣayy ibn Kilāb. Her mother was Salmā daughter of ʿĀmira ibn ʿUmayra ibn Wadīʿa ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Fihr. Her mother was Hind daughter of ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Wāʾila ibn Ḍarb ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿIyādh ibn Yashkur ibn ʿAdwān ibn ʿAmr ibn Qays ʿAylān. Her mother was ʿUmayra daughter of Naṣr ibn ʿĀmir ibn Ḍarb ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿIyādh ibn Yashkur ibn ʿAdwān. Her mother was Zaynab daughter of Naṣr ibn ʿĀmir ibn Sulaym ibn Saʿd ibn Fahm ibn ʿAmr ibn Qays ʿAylān. Her mother was a daughter of Ṣuhba ibn Shabāba ibn ʿAmr ibn Qays ibn Fahm.

Her mother was ʿĀtika daughter of ʿĀmir ibn Ḍarb ibn ʿAmr. Her mother was Shaqīqa daughter of Qutayba ibn Maʿn ibn Mālik ibn Yaʿṣur ibn Saʿd ibn Qays ibn ʿAylān.

Her mother was Sawda daughter of Asīd ibn ʿAmr ibn Tamīm.

He was appointed caliph at the beginning of the year twenty-four, and ruled for twelve years.

At the completion of the year twenty-four, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf al-Zuhrī led the pilgrimage for the people.

ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān ﵀ led the pilgrimage for the people in the years twenty-five, twenty-six, twenty-seven, twenty-eight, twenty-nine, thirty, and thirty-one. ʿUthmān likewise led the pilgrimage for the people in the year thirty-two.

In that year, al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib ﵁ died in the month of Ramaḍān, at the age of eighty-seven or eighty-eight years and seven months. ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān also led the pilgrimage for the people in the years thirty-three and thirty-four.

He was killed on Friday morning, when eighteen nights of Dhū al-Ḥijja had passed; it is also said that two nights remained in Dhū al-Ḥijja. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib led the pilgrimage, having been appointed to do so by ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān. He was killed at the age of eighty-two.

Then ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib was appointed caliph,

ʿAbd Manāf ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib ibn Hāshim ibn ʿAbd Manāf ibn Quṣayy ibn Kilāb—may God’s good pleasure and mercy be upon ʿAlī. His mother was Fāṭima daughter of Asad ibn Hāshim ibn ʿAbd Manāf. Her mother was Fāṭima daughter of Hirm ibn Rawāḥa ibn Ḥujr ibn ʿAbd ibn Maʿīṣ ibn ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy. Her mother was Jadīya daughter of Wahb ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Wāʾila ibn ʿAmr ibn Shaybān ibn Muḥārib ibn Fihr.

Her mother was Fāṭima daughter of ʿUbayd ibn Munqidh ibn ʿAmr ibn Maʿīṣ ibn ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy. Her mother was Salmā daughter of ʿĀmir ibn Rabīʿa ibn Hilāl ibn Ahib ibn Ḍabba ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Fihr. Her mother was ʿĀtika daughter of Abū Humhuma ibn— ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn ʿĀmira ibn ʿUmayra ibn Wadīʿa ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Fihr. Her mother was Tamāḍur daughter of Abū ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd Manāf ibn Quṣayy. Her mother was Amat Allāh, namely Ḥabība daughter of ʿAbd Yālīl ibn Sālim ibn Mālik ibn Ḥuṭayṭ ibn Jusham ibn Qusayy, who was Thaqīf ibn Munabbih. Her mother was Qilāba daughter of Makhzūm ibn Ṣubḥ ibn Wāʾila ibn Fahm ibn ʿAmr ibn Qays.

His caliphate lasted five years less two months. In the year thirty-six, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās performed the pilgrimage for the people on behalf of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵁.

In the year thirty-seven, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib likewise performed it on behalf of ʿAlī. In the year thirty-eight, ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās led the pilgrimage for the people; it is also said that it was Qutham ibn al-ʿAbbās. In the year thirty-nine, Shayba ibn ʿUthmān al-Ḥajabī led the pilgrimage for the people. ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵁ had sent ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās to lead the pilgrimage, but they disagreed. The people reconciled the two, and they agreed on Shayba ibn ʿUthmān.

Then he ﵁ was killed by ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Muljam in the month of Ramaḍān. He struck him two nights before the last ten nights began. He ﵁ died on the first night of the last ten nights of the year forty, at the age of sixty-three.

Then al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ﵄ was appointed caliph,

and his mother was Fāṭima, daughter of the Messenger of God ﷺ and ﵂. Her mother was Khadīja daughter of Khuwaylid ibn Asad ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn Quṣayy. Her mother was Fāṭima daughter of Zāʾida ibn al-Aṣamm ibn Hirm ibn Rawāḥa ibn Ḥujr ibn ʿAbd ibn Maʿīṣ ibn ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy. Her mother was Hāla daughter of ʿAbd Manāf ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Munqidh ibn ʿAmr ibn Maʿīṣ. Her mother was al-ʿAriqa, namely Qilāba daughter of Saʿīd ibn Sahm ibn ʿAmr ibn Huṣayṣ ibn Kaʿb.

Her mother was ʿĀtika daughter of ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn Quṣayy. Her mother was al-Ḥaẓiyā, namely Rayṭa daughter of Kaʿb ibn Saʿd ibn Taym ibn Murra. Her mother was Māwiya daughter of Ḥudhāfa ibn Jumāḥ ibn ʿAmr ibn Huṣayṣ. Her mother was Laylā daughter of ʿĀmir ibn al-Ḥārith, who was Ghubshān ibn ʿAbd ʿAmr ibn Buʾayy ibn Malkān ibn Afṣā, of Khuzāʿa. Her mother was Salmā daughter of Saʿd ibn Kaʿb ibn ʿAmr. Her mother was Laylā daughter of ʿĀʾish ibn Ḍarb ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Fihr. Her mother was Nuʿm daughter of Kaʿb ibn Luʾayy ibn Ghālib. Her mother was Salmā daughter of Muḥārib ibn Fihr. Her mother was ʿĀtika daughter of Yakhlud ibn al-Naḍr ibn Kināna.

Al-Ḥasan remained after his father ﵄ for two months. Then he set out for al-Madāʾin, where Jarāḥ ibn Sinān al-Asadī, one of the Banū Naṣr ibn Quʿayn, stabbed him at Muẓlim Sābāṭ. Then he proceeded until he and Muʿāwiya met and he pledged allegiance to him. Al-Ḥasan ﵁ then died at the age of forty-nine.

Allegiance was pledged to Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān, Ṣakhr ibn Ḥarb ibn Umayya ibn ʿAbd Shams. His mother was Hind daughter of ʿUtba ibn Rabīʿa ibn ʿAbd Shams. Her mother was Ṣafiyya daughter of Umayya ibn Ḥāritha ibn al-Awqaṣ al-Sulamī. Her mother was Āmina daughter of Nawfal ibn ʿAbd Manāf. Her mother was Qilāba daughter of Jābir ibn Naṣr ibn Mālik ibn Ḥisl ibn ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy. Her mother was Tamāḍur daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn Ḥabīb ibn Judhayma ibn Mālik ibn Ḥisl ibn ʿĀmir. Her mother was al-Ṣammāʾ daughter of Saʿīd ibn Sahm ibn ʿAmr. Her mother was ʿĀtika daughter of ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn Quṣayy.

Her mother was al-Ḥaẓiyā, namely Rayṭa daughter of Kaʿb ibn Saʿd ibn Taym ibn Murra.

Her mother was Qayla daughter of Ḥudhāfa ibn Jumāḥ ibn ʿAmr.

Allegiance was pledged to him in the month of Rabīʿ al-Awwal. He ruled for nineteen years and eight months. In the year forty, al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba al-Thaqafī led the pilgrimage for the people. In the year forty-one, ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān led the pilgrimage for the people. Hishām said: “It was only ʿAnbasa ibn Abī Sufyān who led the pilgrimage, not ʿUtba.” In the year forty-two, ʿAnbasa also led the pilgrimage. In the year forty-three, Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam led the pilgrimage for the people. In the year forty-four, Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān led the pilgrimage for the people. In the year forty-five, Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam led the pilgrimage for the people. In the year forty-six, ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān led the pilgrimage for the people. In the year forty-seven, Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam led the pilgrimage; it is also said that it was ʿUtba. In the year forty-eight, Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam led the pilgrimage; it is also said that it was Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ. In the year forty-nine, Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ led the pilgrimage. In the year fifty, Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān led the pilgrimage; it is also said that it was Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya. In the year fifty-one, Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya led the pilgrimage; it is also said that it was Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ. In the year fifty-two, Muʿāwiya led the pilgrimage; it is also said that it was Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ. In the year fifty-three, Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam led the pilgrimage; it is also said that it was Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ. In the year fifty-four, Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ led the pilgrimage; it is also said that it was Marwān. In the year fifty-five, Marwān led the pilgrimage; it is also said that it was ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān. In the year fifty-six, al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān led the pilgrimage for the people.

In the year fifty-seven, al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba led the pilgrimage; it is also said that it was ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān. And in…

In the year fifty-eight, ʿUthmān ibn Muḥammad ibn Abī Sufyān—though it is said, al-Walīd—[led the pilgrimage]. In the year fifty-nine, al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba—though it is said, ʿUthmān ibn Muḥammad.

Then Muʿāwiya died at the new moon of Rajab in the year sixty, aged seventy-seven. His rule had lasted nineteen years and eight months. Of that period, the fitna lasted four years, two months, and twenty-two days.

Then his son assumed authority. His mother was Maysūn, daughter of Baḥdal ibn Unayf ibn Dalja ibn Qunāfa ibn ʿAdī ibn Zuhayr ibn Ḥāritha ibn Janāb ibn Hubl. Her mother was Usayda, daughter of Thaʿlaba ibn Suwayd ibn Asāf ibn ʿAdī ibn Ḥāritha ibn Janāb. Her mother was Ṣaʿba, daughter of Miʿqal ibn ʿAdī ibn Ḥāritha ibn Janāb. Her mother was Qutayla, daughter of Qunāfa ibn ʿAdī ibn Janāb. Her mother was Fakha, daughter of Ubayy ibn Imruʾ al-Qays ibn Zuhayr ibn Janāb. Her mother was Muṣʿaba, daughter of ʿUbayd ibn Ṭarīf ibn Mālik ibn Jadʿā ibn Dhuhl ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Rūmān, of Ṭayyiʾ. Her mother was Ḥubbā, daughter of Janāb ibn Hubl.

In the year sixty, ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ—though it is said, al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba—led the pilgrimage. In the year sixty-one, al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba did so. In the year sixty-two, al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba—though it is said, ʿUthmān ibn Muḥammad ibn Abī Sufyān—did so.

In the year sixty-three, ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr led the pilgrimage. Likewise in the year sixty-four. / Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya died after nineteen nights had passed in Ṣafar of the year sixty-four, aged thirty-six. His rule had lasted three years and eight months. ʿUthmān ibn ʿAnbasa ibn Abī Sufyān performed the funeral prayer over him.

Then Muʿāwiya ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya assumed authority. His mother was Umm Hāshim, whose name was Ḥayya, daughter of Abū Hāshim ibn ʿUtba ibn Rabīʿa ibn ʿAbd Shams. Her mother was Fāṭima, daughter of ʿAbd al-Shāriq ibn Sufyān ibn Qumayr ibn ʿĀmir ibn Rābiya ibn Julayḥa ibn Khaṯʿam. Her mother was Dhabla, daughter of Anas ibn Madrik al-Khaṯʿamī. He ruled for forty days.

The fitna of ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr lasted nine years. The mother of ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr was Asmāʾ, daughter of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq. Her mother was Qutayla, daughter of ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn ʿAbd Asʿad ibn Jābir ibn Mālik ibn Ḥisl ibn ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy. Her mother was Kharmaʾ, daughter of Khalaf ibn Wahb ibn Ḥudhāfa ibn Jumah. Her mother was Lubnā, daughter of ʿAbd ibn Asad ibn Jaḥdam ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Fihr.

Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ ibn Umayya ibn ʿAbd Shams was acclaimed in the year sixty-four, while Ibn al-Zubayr was holding out in Mecca. Marwān’s mother was Āmina, daughter of ʿAlqama ibn Ṣafwān ibn Umayya ibn Muḥarrith ibn Khaml ibn Shiq ibn Raqaba ibn— Mukhdij ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Mālik ibn Kināna. Her mother was al-Ṣaʿba, daughter of Abū Ṭalḥa ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd al-Dār. Her mother was Aranab, daughter of Nimrān. His rule lasted nine months. He died in the month of Ramaḍān, aged sixty-one.

His son ʿAbd al-Malik succeeded him. His mother was ʿĀʾisha, daughter of Muʿāwiya ibn al-Mughīra ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ. Her mother was Fāṭima, daughter of ʿĀmir ibn Ḥudhaym ibn Salāmān ibn Rabīʿa ibn Saʿd ibn Jumah. Her mother was Arwā, daughter of Abū Muʿayṭ ibn Abī ʿAmr ibn Umayya ibn ʿAbd Shams. Her mother was Sālimā, daughter of Abī Umayya ibn Ḥāritha ibn al-Awqaṣ ibn Murra ibn Hilāl ibn Fālij ibn Dhakwān al-Sulamī. Her mother was Umm Muḥammad, daughter of Khuzāʿī ibn Ḥuzāba ibn Murra ibn Hilāl ibn Fālij. Her mother was Nāʾila, daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn Ḥubayb ibn Judhayma ibn Mālik ibn Ḥisl ibn ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy. Her mother was al-Ṣammāʾ, daughter of Saʿīd ibn Sahm. Her mother was ʿĀtika, daughter of ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn Quṣayy. Her mother was al-Ḥuẓayāriṭa, daughter of Kaʿb ibn Saʿd ibn Taym ibn Murra. Her mother was Qāʾila, daughter of Ḥudhāfa ibn Jumah. Her mother was Umayna, daughter of al-Ḥārith al-Jān, who was al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd ʿAmr ibn Buʾayy ibn Malkān. Her mother was Salmā, daughter of Saʿd ibn Kaʿb ibn ʿAmr ibn Khuzāʿa. Her mother was Laylā, daughter of ʿĀʾish ibn Ḍarb ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Fihr. Her mother was Salmā, daughter of Luʾayy ibn Ghālib. Her mother was Laylā, daughter of Muḥārib ibn Fihr. Her mother was ʿĀtika, daughter of Yakhlud ibn al-Naḍr ibn Kināna.

In the year sixty-five, ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr led the pilgrimage for the people.

He did so likewise in the years sixty-six, sixty-seven, sixty-eight, sixty-nine, seventy, and seventy-one. In the year seventy, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ﵁—died.

In the years seventy-two and seventy-three, al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf ibn al-Ḥakam ibn Abī ʿAqīl al-Thaqafī led the pilgrimage for the people. In that year, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb died. In that year, ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr was killed on Tuesday, after seventeen days of Jumādā I had passed—though it is said, on its midpoint—aged seventy-three. It is said that in the year seventy-two al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf and those with him led the pilgrimage and stayed at Minā1 They did not enter Mecca or circumambulate the House, while Ibn al-Zubayr was in Mecca.

After Ibn al-Zubayr was killed, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān ruled for thirteen years and eight months. In the year seventy-four, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān led the pilgrimage—though it is said, al-Ḥajjāj.

/ In the year seventy-five, Abān ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān led the pilgrimage—though it is said, al-Ḥajjāj. In the years seventy-six, seventy-seven, seventy-eight, and seventy-nine, ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān did so—though it is said, Abān ibn ʿUthmān. In the year eighty, Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān did so—though it is said, Abān ibn ʿUthmān. In the year eighty-one, Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik did so. In the year eighty-two, Abān ibn ʿUthmān did so. In the year eighty-four, Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl did so. ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān died on the tenth of Shawwāl in the year eighty-six, aged sixty-two.

Al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān succeeded him. His mother and Sulaymān’s mother were one and the same; her name was Walīda. She was Umm al-Walīd, daughter of al-ʿAbbās ibn Juzayy ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Zuhayr ibn Judhayma ibn Rawāḥa ibn Rabīʿa ibn Māzin ibn Quṭayʿa ibn ʿAbs. Her mother was Zaynab, daughter of Qays ibn Abī ʿAmra ibn Usāma ibn Ḥudhaym ibn Judhayma. Her mother was Salmā, daughter of Harthama ibn Qumāma ibn Usāma ibn Ḥudhaym.

Her mother was Ḥabība, daughter of al-Dafʾ, of ʿAbd al-Qays.

He ruled for nine years and nine months. In the year eighty-seven, Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl led the pilgrimage for the people by order of ʿAbd al-Malik, and al-Walīd carried out that order.

In the year eighty-eight, ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz led the pilgrimage—though it is said, ʿUmar ibn al-Walīd. In the year eighty-nine, ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz did so. In the years ninety and ninety-one, ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz did so—though it is said, al-Walīd. In the year ninety-two, al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik did so—though it is said, ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Walīd, and this is more accurate.

In the year ninety-three, ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz did so—though it is said, al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

In the year ninety-four, Bishr ibn al-Walīd did so—though it is said, al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik; it is also said, ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Walīd, or Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. In the year ninety-five, Bishr ibn al-Walīd did so. / Al-Walīd died on Saturday, after fourteen nights of Jumādā II had passed, in the year ninety-six, aged forty-three.

Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik succeeded him. His mother was Umm al-Walīd. He ruled for three years. In the year ninety-six, Abū Bakr ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm led the pilgrimage for the people—though it is said, Maslama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. In the year ninety-seven, Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik did so. In the year ninety-eight, Abū Bakr ibn Ḥazm al-Anṣārī did so—though it is said, ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Usayd. In the year ninety-nine, Abū Bakr ibn Ḥazm did so. In that year Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik died on Friday—though it is said, on Thursday—after ten days of Ṣafar had passed; it is also said, after five days of Rabīʿ I had passed. His rule had lasted two years and nine months or eight months and five days. He was thirty-nine years old.

ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam succeeded him.

His mother was Umm ʿĀṣim, daughter of ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb. Her mother was Umm ʿAmmār, daughter of Sufyān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rabīʿa ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Ḥabīb ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Mālik ibn Ḥuṭayṭ ibn Jusham ibn Thaqīf. Her mother was Zaynab, daughter of ʿAlāq ibn Shihāb ibn ʿAmr, of Banū ʿAwāfa ibn Saʿd ibn Zayd Manāt. Her mother was Umm ʿĀṣim, daughter of Sinān ibn Khālid ibn Minqar ibn ʿUbayd ibn al-Ḥārith—who was Muqāʿis—ibn ʿAmr ibn Kaʿb ibn Saʿd ibn Zayd Manāt ibn Tamīm. Her mother was Kabsha, daughter of ʿAmr ibn Usayyid ibn Jaḥwān ibn Kaʿb ibn ʿAbshams ibn Jusham ibn Saʿd ibn Zayd Manāt ibn Tamīm. Her mother was Manẓūra, daughter of Fadakī ibn Aʿbad ibn Saʿd ibn Minqar. Her mother was Rabīʿa, daughter of Khālid ibn ʿAbshams ibn Jusham ibn Saʿd. Her mother was Khumāʿa, daughter of Jundub ibn ʿAwf ibn Saʿd ibn Zayd Manāt ibn Tamīm.

He assumed authority at Dābiq on Friday, after ten days of Ṣafar had passed, in the year ninety-nine. In the year one hundred, Abū Bakr ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥazm led the pilgrimage. In the year one hundred and one, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays al-Fihrī did so—though it is said, ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿUmar. ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz died at Dayr Simʿān, in the district of Ḥimṣ, six days before the end of Rajab in the year one hundred and one.

His rule had lasted thirty months. He was thirty-nine years old.

Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān succeeded him. His mother was ʿĀtika, daughter of Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān. Her mother was Umm Kulthūm, daughter of ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Kurayz ibn Rabīʿa ibn Ḥabīb ibn ʿAbd Shams. Her mother was Amat Allāh, daughter of al-Wārith ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Rabīʿa ibn Khuwaylid ibn Nufayl ibn ʿAmr ibn Kilāb. Her mother was Zaynab, daughter of Zuhayr ibn Judhayma ibn Rawāḥa al-ʿAbsī.

In the year one hundred and two, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays al-Fihrī led the pilgrimage. He did so likewise in the year one hundred and three. In the year one hundred and four, ʿAbd al-Wāḥid ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Naṣrī, of Banū Naṣr ibn Muʿāwiya ibn Bakr ibn Hawāzin ibn Manṣūr, led the pilgrimage. Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn— Marwān died, and his son al-Walīd performed the funeral prayer over him at al-Balqāʾ, in the district of Damascus, four days before the end of Shaʿbān in the year one hundred and five. His rule had lasted four years and one month.

He died aged thirty-seven.

Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān succeeded him. His mother, Umm Hishām, was named Fāṭima, daughter of Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl ibn Hishām ibn al-Walīd ibn al-Walīd ibn al-Mughīra. Her mother was Maryam, daughter of Lajāʾ ibn ʿAwf ibn Ḥāritha ibn Sinān ibn Abī Ḥāritha ibn Murra ibn Nashba. Her mother was Saʿdā, daughter of Qays ibn Khārija ibn Sinān ibn Abī Ḥāritha ibn Murra ibn Nashba. Her mother was Mulayka, daughter of ʿAwf ibn Abī Ḥāritha. Her mother was Ṣafiyya, daughter of Sufyān ibn Rabīʿa ibn al-Ashyam ibn al-Asʿar ibn Ḍarba ibn Ṣirma ibn ʿAwf ibn Saʿd ibn Dhubyān.

In the year one hundred and five, Ibrāhīm ibn Hishām al-Makhzūmī led the pilgrimage for the people.

In the year one hundred and six, Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik did so. In the years one hundred and seven, one hundred and eight, one hundred and nine, one hundred and ten, one hundred and eleven, and one hundred and twelve, Ibrāhīm ibn Hishām al-Makhzūmī did so.

In the year one hundred and thirteen, Sulaymān ibn Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik did so. In the year one hundred and fourteen, Khālid ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn al-Ḥārith ibn al-Ḥakam ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ did so.

In the year one hundred and fifteen, al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik did so. In the year one hundred and seventeen— Khālid ibn ʿAbd al-Malik did so. In the year one hundred and eighteen, Muḥammad ibn Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl did so.

In the year one hundred and nineteen, Sulaymān ibn Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik did so. In the year one hundred and twenty, Muḥammad ibn Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl did so. In the year one hundred and twenty-one, Muḥammad ibn Hishām did so as well. He did so likewise in the years one hundred and twenty-two, one hundred and twenty-three, and one hundred and twenty-four. In the year one hundred and twenty-four, Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib ﵀—died. Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān died at al-Ruṣāfa on Wednesday, after seven days of Rabīʿ I had passed, in the year one hundred and twenty-five, aged fifty-three. His rule had lasted twenty years less five months.

Then al-Walīd ibn Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān succeeded him. His mother was Umm al-Ḥajjāj, daughter of Muḥammad ibn Yūsuf ibn al-Ḥakam ibn Abī ʿAqīl ibn Masʿūd ibn ʿĀmir ibn Muʿattib ibn Mālik ibn Kaʿb ibn ʿAmr ibn Saʿd ibn ʿAwf ibn Qasī, who was Thaqīf. Her mother was Umm ʿUthmān, daughter of Salmā ibn Nawfal ibn Muʿāwiya son of ʿUrwa ibn Ṣakhr ibn Yaʿmar ibn Nufātha ibn ʿAdī ibn al-Diʾl ibn Bakr ibn ʿAbd Manāt ibn Kināna. Her mother was Zaynab daughter of ʿUwaydh ibn Kaʿb ibn al-Ḥulays ibn Rawāḥa ibn Nufātha.

In the year 125, Yūsuf ibn Muḥammad ibn Yūsuf al-Thaqafī led the pilgrimage. Al-Walīd ibn Yazīd was killed at al-Bakhr2 on the remaining five days of Jumādā II in the year 126. He was forty years old.

His rule lasted one year and three months. The one who killed him was ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Ḥajjāj ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān, whom Yazīd al-Nāqiṣ ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik had sent against him. Al-Nāqiṣ was a Muʿtazilī.

Yazīd ibn al-Walīd al-Nāqiṣ seized power. His mother was Shāhfarīdh, daughter of Fīrūz ibn Kisrā Yazdajird ibn Shahrīyār ibn Kisrā Abrīwīz ibn Hurmuz ibn Anūshirwān ibn Qubādh ibn Fīrūz ibn Yazdajird.

Her mother was Rayḥāna, daughter of Shīrūya ibn Kisrā Abrīwīz. Shīrūya’s mother was Maryam, daughter of the Caesar. Fīrūz’s mother was the daughter of the king of the Turks. Yazīd was the one who said:

I am the son of Kisrā of Fārs and Marwān,

And Caesar is my grandfather, as is Khāqān.

Yazīd assumed power at the beginning of Rajab, five days after the killing of al-Walīd ibn Yazīd, in the year 126. He ruled for five months, the last of which ended on the final day of Dhū al-Qaʿda. He died at the age of thirty-three. His brother Ibrāhīm ibn al-Walīd, the deposed, performed the funeral prayer over him.

After Yazīd, his brother Ibrāhīm ibn al-Walīd ruled for seventy days. Then he was deposed in Damascus. His mother was a concubine. It is said that Ibrāhīm remained in power for four months, then was deposed in Rabīʿ II of the year 127. Marwān ibn Muḥammad ibn Marwān came with the people of the Jazīra and encountered Bishr and Marwān, the sons of al-Walīd. He captured them and routed their army. Ibrāhīm’s defeat took place in Ṣafar.

He remained in power until he was killed in the year 132, among those of the Umayyads who were killed. In the year 126, ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān led the pilgrimage for the people; it is also said that it was ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz.

Marwān held power for five years. His mother was a Kurdish concubine. This was in the year 127. In the year 128, ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, Marwān ibn Muḥammad’s governor, led the pilgrimage.

In the year 129, ʿAbd al-Wāḥid ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān led the pilgrimage. In the year 130, Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn ʿAṭiyya, one of the Banū Saʿd ibn Bakr ibn Hawāzin, led the pilgrimage. In the year 131, al-Walīd ibn ʿUrwa al-Saʿdī, of Saʿd ibn Bakr ibn Hawāzin, led the pilgrimage.

This was the last pilgrimage performed under the rule of the Umayyads.

Then the Banū Hāshim came to power. In the year 132, Dāwūd ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās led the pilgrimage. Marwān ibn Muḥammad was killed at the end of Dhū al-Ḥijja at Būṣīr. He was sixty-nine years old. He was killed in the year 133.

Abū al-ʿAbbās was acclaimed caliph—his name was [al-Saffāḥ] ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib—on the night of Friday, thirteen nights having passed of Rabīʿ II in the year 132. His mother was Rayṭa, daughter of ʿUbayd ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Madān ibn al-Dayyān, one of the Banū al-Ḥārith ibn Kaʿb.

Her mother was Ḥasnāʾ, daughter of Saʿīd ibn Zayd ibn al-Aswad ibn al-Muḥajjal ibn Ḥazn ibn Muʾāla. Her mother was Umm Yazīd, daughter of ʿĀbis ibn Nabāta ibn Zayd ibn al-Ḍibāb. Her mother was Fāṭima, daughter of al-ʿAtīr ibn Rabīʿa ibn Muʿāwiya ibn Ṣalāʾ ibn al-Miʿqal, ibn Kaʿb ibn Rabīʿa ibn Kaʿb ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Kaʿb. Her mother was Malīka, daughter of Muʿāwiya ibn Qays ibn Kaʿb ibn Miʿqal. Her mother was Zaynab, daughter of Jandal ibn Durayd ibn Sufyān ibn al-Ḍibāb.

In the year 133, Ziyād ibn ʿUbayd Allāh al-Ḥārithī led the pilgrimage for the people. In the year 134, ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās did so. In the year 135, Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās did so. Abū al-ʿAbbās died at al-Anbār on Sunday, twelve nights having passed of Dhū al-Ḥijja in the year 136. He was thirty-six years old.

His caliphate lasted four years, eight months, and four days. For eight of those months he was fighting Marwān ibn Muḥammad. ʿĪsā ibn ʿAlī performed the funeral prayer over him.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās, al-Manṣūr, was designated as successor.

His mother was a Berber concubine named Sallāma. In the year 136, al-Manṣūr led the pilgrimage for the people. In the year 137, Ismāʿīl ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās did so. In the year 138, al-Faḍl ibn Ṣāliḥ ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās did so. In the year 139, al-ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās did so. In the year 140, al-Manṣūr did so. In the year 141, Ṣāliḥ ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh did so. In the year 142, Ismāʿīl ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh did so. In the year 143, ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī did so. In the year 144, al-Manṣūr did so.

In [the year] 145, al-Sirrī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith did so.

In that year, Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥasan ibn Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib revolted in Medina and was killed in Ramaḍān of that year. His brother Ibrāhīm revolted in Iraq and was killed on the tenth of Dhū al-Ḥijja at Bākhamrā. In the year 146, ʿAbd al-Wahhāb ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad led the pilgrimage. In the year 147, al-Manṣūr did so. In the year 148, Jaʿfar ibn al-Manṣūr did so. In the year 149, Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī did so. In the year 150, ʿAbd al-Ṣamad ibn ʿAlī did so. In the year 151, Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī did so. In the year 152, al-Manṣūr did so. In the year 153, al-Mahdī ibn al-Manṣūr did so. In the year 154, Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī did so. In the year 155, ʿAbd al-Ṣamad ibn ʿAlī did so.

In the year 156, al-ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī did so. In the year 157, Ibrāhīm ibn Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad did so. In the year 158, Ibrāhīm ibn Yaḥyā ibn Muḥammad did so again. Abū Jaʿfar al-Manṣūr died in Mecca on the remaining six days of Dhū al-Ḥijja in the year 158. ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā ibn Muḥammad performed the funeral prayer over him. His caliphate lasted twenty-two years, less twenty-four days.

He died at the age of sixty-eight.

Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Manṣūr, al-Mahdī, was designated as successor. His mother was Umm Mūsā, daughter of Manṣūr ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Shahr ibn Yazīd ibn Mathwūb ibn al-Ashhal ibn Mathwūb ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Shammir Dhū al-Janāḥ ibn Luhayʿa Yanʿum ibn Yaʿfur Yankuf ibn Fahdī Dhū ʿAsīm ibn Aʿrab Yankuf ibn Ghaydān ibn Yarīm Dhū Ruʿayn ibn Zayd ibn Sahl ibn ʿAmr ibn Qays ibn Muʿāwiya ibn Jusham ibn ʿAbd Shams ibn Wāʾil ibn al-Ghawth ibn Qaṭan ibn ʿArīb ibn Zuhayr ibn Ayman ibn al-Hamīsaʿ ibn Ḥimyar ibn Sabaʾ. Her mother was a concubine. Allegiance was pledged to al-Mahdī on Saturday, with six nights remaining in Dhū al-Ḥijja. Allegiance was pledged to him in Baghdad on Thursday, with eleven nights remaining in Dhū al-Ḥijja. In the year 159, Yazīd ibn Manṣūr al-Ḥimyarī led the pilgrimage. In the year 160, the Commander of the Faithful, al-Mahdī, did so. He ordered the Sacred Mosque to be enlarged, had the covering of the Kaʿba stripped off, and covered it anew. In the year 161, Mūsā ibn al-Mahdī led the pilgrimage. In the year 162, Ibrāhīm ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Jaʿfar did so. In the year 163, ʿAlī ibn al-Mahdī did so. In the year 164, Ṣāliḥ ibn Abī Jaʿfar did so.

Al-Mahdī turned back from al-ʿAqaba because thirst afflicted the people. In the year 165, Ṣāliḥ ibn Abī Jaʿfar led the pilgrimage. In the year 166, Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī did so. In the year 167, Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm did so again. In the year 168, ʿAlī ibn al-Mahdī did so. Al-Mahdī died with eight days remaining of Muḥarram in the year 169, at the age of forty-three. His caliphate lasted ten years, one month, and twenty-two days.

Mūsā, al-Hādī, was designated as successor. His mother, and the mother of Hārūn al-Rashīd, was al-Khayzurān, a concubine. Al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Ḥasan ibn Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib revolted against him in Medina in Dhū al-Qaʿda and set out for Mecca. He was met there by al-ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad, Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā, and Muḥammad ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī. He was killed at Fakh one day before the Day of Tarwiya. In the year 169, Sulaymān ibn al-Manṣūr led the pilgrimage. Mūsā al-Hādī died at ʿĪsābādh on the night of Friday, fourteen nights having passed of Rabīʿ I in the year 170, at the age of twenty-six. His caliphate lasted fourteen months.

Hārūn al-Rashīd was designated as successor. His mother was al-Khayzurān, a concubine.

In the year 170, Hārūn al-Rashīd led the pilgrimage. In the year 171, ʿAbd al-Ṣamad ibn ʿAlī did so. In the year 172, Yaʿqūb ibn al-Manṣūr did so. In the years 173, 174, and 175, Hārūn al-Rashīd did so. In the year 176, Sulaymān ibn Abī Jaʿfar did so. In the year 177, al-Rashīd did so. In the year 178, Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad did so. In the year 179, al-Rashīd did so. In the year 180, Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā did so. In the year 181, al-Rashīd did so.

He went to al-Raqqa and settled there. In the year 182, Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā led the pilgrimage. In the year 183, al-ʿAbbās ibn Mūsā al-Hādī did so. In the year 184, Ibrāhīm ibn al-Mahdī did so. In the year 185, Manṣūr ibn al-Mahdī did so. In the year 186, al-Rashīd did so, accompanied by al-Amīn and al-Maʾmūn. He gave the people of Medina three stipends and the people of Mecca two stipends. He drew up the two documents of appointment for al-Amīn and al-Maʾmūn, and they were hung in the Kaʿba.

In the years 187 and 188, al-Rashīd led the pilgrimage. In the year 187, he became angry with the Barmakids. He gave the people of Medina half a stipend; this was his last pilgrimage. In the year 189, al-ʿAbbās ibn Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā led the pilgrimage. In the year 190, ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā al-Hādī did so.

In the year 191, al-Faḍl ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī led the pilgrimage. In the year 192, al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar ibn al-Manṣūr did so.

He surpassed the others through the excellence of his deeds. He brought one thousand foot soldiers and distributed thirty thousand among them, giving each foot soldier thirty dirhams. He distributed between one thousand and two thousand among the Banū Shayba. He carried out benefactions such as none who had held this office had carried out during the pilgrimage season.

Al-Rashīd died at Ṭūs in Khurāsān on Saturday, the first of Jumādā II; it is also said, on the night of Sunday, the first of Jumādā II; and it is also said, on the remaining three days of Jumādā II in the year 193. He was forty-five years old. Ṣāliḥ ibn al-Rashīd performed the funeral prayer over him. News of his death reached Baghdad on Wednesday, with twelve nights remaining in Jumādā II. Muḥammad al-Amīn announced his death to the people on Friday. His caliphate lasted twenty-three years, one month, and sixteen days.

Muḥammad ibn al-Rashīd, al-Amīn, was designated as successor on Thursday, with eleven nights remaining in Jumādā II. His mother was Zubayda, daughter of Jaʿfar ibn al-Manṣūr. In the year 193, Dāwūd ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā led the pilgrimage for the people. In the year 194, ʿAlī ibn al-Rashīd did so. In the year 195, Dāwūd ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā did so. In the year 196, al-ʿAbbās ibn Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā led the pilgrimage. Likewise in the year 197.

Al-Amīn was killed on the night of Sunday, with five nights having passed of Muḥarram in the year 198. He was twenty-four years and some months old. It is also said that he was twenty-eight years old less one month. His caliphate lasted four years, seven months, and six days. Ibn al-Kalbī said: four years, eight months, and five days. He also said that al-Rashīd died in Jumādā I and Muḥammad was killed with six nights remaining of Muḥarram.

ʿAbd Allāh ibn Hārūn, al-Maʾmūn, was designated as successor with five nights having passed of Muḥarram in the year 198. His mother was a concubine called Marājil. In the year 198, al-ʿAbbās ibn Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā led the pilgrimage for the people. In the year 199, Ibn al-Afṭas did so. Sulaymān ibn Dāwūd ibn ʿĪsā had set out to lead the pilgrimage, but Ibn al-Afṭas rose up, placed the pilgrimage encampment at Minā, and seized control of the pilgrimage season.

Sulaymān withdrew and did not proceed to ʿArafāt. It is said that the people stood there and departed without an imam. In the year 200, Abū Isḥāq ibn Hārūn al-Rashīd led the pilgrimage. In the year 201, Isḥāq ibn Mūsā ibn ʿĪsā did so. In the year 202, Ibrāhīm ibn Mūsā ibn Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib did so. In the year 203, Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd Allāh/ b. Sulaymān b. ʿAlī. In the year 204, ʿUbayd Allāh b. al-Ḥasan b. ʿUbayd Allāh b. al-ʿAbbās b. ʿAlī b. Abī Ṭālib [led the pilgrimage]. Likewise in the years 205 and 206. In the year 207, Abū ʿĪsā b. al-Rashīd led the pilgrimage. In the year 208, Ṣāliḥ b. al-Rashīd [led it]. In the year 209, Ṣāliḥ b. al-ʿAbbās b. Muḥammad b. ʿAlī [led it]. Likewise in the years 210 and 211. In the year 212, ʿAbd Allāh b. ʿUbayd Allāh b. al-ʿAbbās b. Muḥammad led the pilgrimage. Likewise in the year 213.

In the year 214, Isḥāq b. al-ʿAbbās b. Muḥammad led the pilgrimage.

In the year 215, ʿAbd Allāh b. ʿUbayd Allāh b. al-ʿAbbās b. Muḥammad [led it].

Likewise in the year 216. In the year 217, Sulaymān b. ʿAbd Allāh b. Sulaymān b. ʿAlī led it. Al-Maʾmūn died on Wednesday, eight nights having passed of Rajab, in the year 218, at al-Badhandōn. He was buried at Ṭarsūs, and his brother Abū Isḥāq performed the funeral prayer over him. His caliphate lasted twenty years, five months, and thirteen days. He died at the age of forty-nine years, two months, and eighteen days. He was born at the midpoint of the month of Rabīʿ II in the year 170.

Muḥammad b. Hārūn al-Rashīd, al-Muʿtaṣim, was invested with the succession. His mother was a concubine called Māridah. He was thirty-nine years old. In the year 218, Ṣāliḥ b. al-ʿAbbās b. Muḥammad led the pilgrimage for the people. Likewise in the years 219 and 220. In the years 221, 222, 223, 224, 225, and 226, Muḥammad b. Dāwūd b. ʿĪsā b. Mūsā led the pilgrimage. Al-Muʿtaṣim died on Thursday, eleven nights remaining of Rabīʿ I in the year 227, at Surr man Raʾā. His son Hārūn al-Wāthiq performed the funeral prayer over him. His caliphate lasted eight years, eight months, and ten days. He died at the age of forty-seven years, seven months, and nineteen days.

Hārūn, al-Wāthiq b. al-Muʿtaṣim, was invested with the succession. His mother was a concubine called Qarāṭīs. In the year 227, Jaʿfar b. al-Muʿtaṣim led the pilgrimage. In the years 228, 229, 230, 231, and 232, Muḥammad b. Dāwūd b. ʿĪsā b. Mūsā led it. Al-Wāthiq died on Wednesday, six nights remaining of Dhū al-Ḥijjah in the year 232, at the age of thirty-seven years, nine months, and thirteen days. His caliphate lasted five years, nine months, and thirteen days.

Jaʿfar, al-Mutawakkil b. al-Muʿtaṣim, was invested with the succession, on Wednesday, six nights remaining of Dhū al-Ḥijjah in the year 232. His mother was a concubine called Shujāʿ. He was twenty-six years, two months, and twelve days old; it is also said that he was twenty-eight years old. He was born in Shawwāl in the year 205. In the years 233, 234, and 235, Muḥammad b. Dāwūd b. ʿĪsā b. Mūsā led the pilgrimage. In the year 236, al-Muntaṣir b. al-Mutawakkil led it. In the year 238.

In the year 239, ʿAbd Allāh b. Muḥammad b. Dāwūd b. ʿĪsā b. Mūsā led it. Likewise in the years 240 and 241.

In the years 242, 243, and 244, ʿAbd al-Ṣamad b. Mūsā b. Muḥammad b. Ibrāhīm b. Muḥammad led it. In the year 245, Muḥammad b. Sulaymān b. Muḥammad b. Ibrāhīm b. Muḥammad b. ʿAlī b. ʿAbd Allāh b. al-ʿAbbās, known as al-Zaynabī, led the pilgrimage. He also led it in the year 246. Al-Mutawakkil was killed on Monday, two nights having passed of Shawwāl in the year 247.

The duration of his caliphate was fourteen [perhaps: fourteen] years and nine months. Abū al-ʿAbbās b. al-Wāthiq said: “Al-Mutawakkil’s mother was a Turkic woman, and she was Mūsā b. Bughā’s maternal aunt. She was called Shujāʿ. The name of her sister—[Mūsā b. Bughā’s mother]—was Ḥasan.”

His son al-Muntaṣir succeeded him; he reigned for six months and died.

Al-Mustaʿīn succeeded him; he reigned for four years less three months, then was deposed.

Al-Muʿtazz succeeded him; he reigned for three years and nine months, then was killed.

Al-Muʿtazz’s mother was an Andalusian woman named Qabīḥah.

Al-Muhtadī succeeded him. Abū ʿAbd Allāh Muḥammad b. Hārūn al-Wāthiq, who was al-Muhtadī, was killed on the sixth of Rajab in the year 256.

His rule lasted eleven months. During his caliphate, the Khārijite rebel rose in Baṣrah in Ramaḍān of the year 256.

Al-Muʿtamid assumed the rule. His mother was Fityān. He was born on Sunday, six days having passed of Rajab in the year 256. His kunya was Abū al-ʿAbbās. He reigned for twenty-three years.

Al-Muʿtaḍid assumed the rule; his mother was a concubine called Ḍirār. This was in Rajab of the year 279. Abū Saʿīd al-Sukkarī said: “Muḥammad b. Ḥabīb Abū Jaʿfar told me all of this.”

Caliphs descended from Qurashī women

Abū Bakr, ʿUmar, ʿUthmān, and ʿAlī ﵃.

Al-Ḥasan b. ʿAlī; Muʿāwiyah b. Ṣakhr; Muʿāwiyah b. Yazīd b. Muʿāwiyah; ʿAbd Allāh b. al-Zubayr; ʿAbd al-Malik b. Marwān; ʿUmar b. ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz; Yazīd b. ʿAbd al-Malik b. Marwān; Hishām b. ʿAbd al-Malik; and Muḥammad b. Hārūn al-Rashīd.

Caliphs descended from Arab women

Yazīd b. Muʿāwiyah; Marwān b. al-Ḥakam; al-Walīd and Sulaymān, sons of ʿAbd al-Malik; and al-Walīd b. Yazīd b. ʿAbd al-Malik.

Abū al-ʿAbbās ʿAbd Allāh b. Muḥammad b. ʿAlī; and al-Mahdī Muḥammad b. ʿAbd Allāh.

Caliphs descended from concubines

Yazīd b. al-Walīd b. ʿAbd al-Malik b. Marwān, whose mother was Persian.

Ibrāhīm b. al-Walīd, whose mother was a concubine; Marwān b. Muḥammad, whose mother was Kurdish; Abū Jaʿfar al-Manṣūr, whose mother was Berber; Mūsā and Hārūn, whose mother was from Jurash; and al-Maʾmūn, whose mother was from Bādhghīs.

Al-Muʿtaṣim; al-Wāthiq; al-Mutawakkil; al-Muntaṣir;

al-Mustaʿīn; al-Muʿtazz; al-Muhtadī; al-Muʿtamid.

And al-Muʿtaḍid bi-llāh b. Abī al-ʿAbbās.

Those who resembled the Prophet ﷺ

Al-Ḥasan b. ʿAlī b. Abī Ṭālib. When Fāṭimah—may God’s blessings be upon her—rocked him, she would say:

O, for my father! He resembles my father,

Though he does not resemble ʿAlī.

Jaʿfar b. Abī Ṭālib; Qutham b. al-ʿAbbās b. ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. Al-ʿAbbās used to rock him and say:

O my son! O Qutham,

O likeness of the generous one!

Muḥammad b. Jaʿfar b. Abī Ṭālib; al-Mughīrah, Abū Sufyān b. al-Ḥārith b. ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, who was born on the night on which the Messenger of God ﷺ was born; and ʿAbd Allāh b. Nawfal b. al-Ḥārith b. ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

Muslim b. Muʿtab b. Abī Lahab; al-Sāʾib b. ʿAbd Yazīd b. al-Muṭṭalib b. Hāshim b. ʿAbd Manāf; and Kābis b. Rabīʿah b. Mālik b. ʿAdī b. al-Aswad b. Ḥashm b. Rabīʿah b. al-Ḥārith b. Sāmah b. Luʾayy.

It reached Muʿāwiyah b. Abī Sufyān that in Baṣrah there was a man who resembled the Messenger of God, God bless him and grant him peace. He wrote to his governor there, ʿAbd Allāh b. ʿĀmir b. Kurayz, ordering him to send the man to him. So he sent Kābis. When Kābis entered before Muʿāwiyah, Muʿāwiyah got down from his throne and walked to him, then kissed him between the eyes and granted him al-Marghāb.

The ʿAwātik who gave birth to the Messenger of God ﷺ

Two were from Quraysh; one was from Banū Yakhlud b. al-Naḍr b. Kinānah; three were from Banū Sulaym; two from ʿAdwān; and one each from Asad, Hudhayl, Quḍāʿah, and Azd. As for the two Qurashī women, they gave birth to him through Asad b. ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā.

Asad was his maternal ancestor through his mother, Āminah bt. Wahb. Āminah’s mother was Barrah bt. ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā b. ʿUthmān b. ʿAbd al-Dār. Her mother was Umm Ḥabīb bt. Asad b. ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā.

Asad’s mother was al-Ḥaẓiyyā—Rīṭah bt. Kaʿb b. Saʿd b. Taym b. Murrah.

Her mother was Qaylah bt. Ḥudhāfah b. Jumah. Her mother was Umaymah bt. ʿĀmir al-Jān b. al-Ḥārith, who was Ghābshān, from Khuzāʿah. Her mother was ʿĀtikah bt. Hilāl b. Uhayb b. Ḍabbah b. al-Ḥārith b. Fihr. Hilāl b. Uhayb’s mother was Hind bt. Hilāl b. ʿĀmir b. Ṣaʿṣaʿah. Ḍabbah b. Uhayb’s mother was ʿĀtikah bt. Ghālib b. Fihr.

Her mother was ʿĀtikah bt. Yakhlud b. al-Naḍr b. Kinānah.

As for the Sulaymī women, they gave birth to him through Hāshim. Hāshim b. ʿAbd Manāf’s mother was ʿĀtikah bt. Murrah b. Hilāl b. Fālij b. Dhakwān b. Thaʿlabah b. Bahthah b. Sulaym b. Manṣūr. Murrah b. Hilāl’s mother was ʿĀtikah bt. Murrah b. ʿAdī b. Aslam b. Afṣā, the father of Khuzāʿah. It is also said that she was ʿĀtikah bt. Jābir b. Qunfudh b. Mālik b. ʿAwf b. Imruʾ al-Qays b. Bahthah b. Sulaym b. Manṣūr. Hilāl b. Fālij’s mother was ʿĀtikah bt. ʿAṣiyyah b. Khafāf b. Imruʾ al-Qays b. Bahthah.

The Hudhalī woman was ʿĀtikah bt. Saʿd b. Hudhayl, who was the mother of ʿAbd Allāh b. Razzām b. Rabīʿah b. Jaḥūsh b. Muʿāwiyah b. Bakr b. Hawāzin. ʿAbd Allāh b. Razzām was the grandfather of ʿAmr b. ʿĀʾidh b. ʿImrān b. Makhzūm, the father of his mother Fāṭimah, who was the second of the Fāṭimahs who gave birth to him ﷺ.

The two ʿAdwānī women gave birth to him ﷺ through his father.

ʿAbd Allāh b. ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib’s mother was Fāṭimah bt. ʿAmr b. ʿĀʾidh b. ʿImrān b. Makhzūm. Her mother was Ṣakhrā bt. ʿImrān b. Makhzūm. Her mother was Takhmur bt. ʿAbd b. Quṣayy. Her mother was Salmā bt. ʿĀmir b. ʿUmayrah/ b. Wadīʿah b. al-Ḥārith b. Fihr. Her mother was Hind bt. ʿAbd Allāh b. al-Ḥārith b. Wāʾilah b. Ḍarb b. ʿAdwān. Her mother was Zaynab bt. Naṣr b. ʿĀmir b. Saʿd b. Qayn b. Fahm b. ʿAmr b. Qays b. ʿAylān. It is also said that she was Zaynab bt. Mālik b. Nāṣirah b. Kaʿb b. Ḥarb b. Saʿd b. Fahm. Her mother was ʿĀtikah bt. ʿĀmir b. al-Ḍarb b. ʿAmr b. ʿIyādh b. Yashkur b. al-Ḥārith, who was ʿAdwān. Her mother was Shaqīqah bt. Qutaybah b. Maʿn b. Mālik b. Yaʿṣur. Her mother was Sūrah bt. Asīd b. ʿAmr b. Tamīm b. Murrah.

Mālik b. al-Naḍr’s mother was ʿĀtikah, also called ʿAkrashah and al-Ḥaṣān, bt. ʿAdwān b. ʿAmr b. Qays. Her mother was Māwiyah bt. Suwayd b. al-Ghuṭrayf, who was Ḥārithah b. Imruʾ al-Qays b. Māzin b. al-Azd b. al-Ghawth.

Al-Naḍr b. Kinānah’s mother was Barrah bt. Murrah b. Add. Her mother was Māwiyah, from Banū Ḍubayʿah b. Rabīʿah b. Nizār. Her mother was ʿĀtikah bt. al-Azd b. al-Ghawth.

As for the Quḍāʿī woman, she gave birth to him ﷺ through Kaʿb b. Luʾayy b. Ghālib. His mother was Māwiyah bt. al-Qayn b. Jisr b. Shayʿ Allāh b. Asad b. Wabrah. Her mother was Waḥshiyyah bt. Rabīʿah b. Ḥarām b. Ḍannah b. ʿAbd b. Kathīr b. ʿUdhrah. Her mother was ʿĀtikah bt. Rashdān b. Qays b.

Juhayna ibn Zayd ibn Layth ibn Sūd ibn Aslam ibn al-Ḥāf ibn Quḍāʿa.

The Asadī woman bore him ﷺ—through Kilāb ibn Murra. His mother was Hind bt. Sarīra ibn Thaʿlaba ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Mālik ibn Kināna.

Her mother was ʿĀtika bt. Dūdān ibn Asad ibn Khuzayma. Her mother was Jadīla bt. Ṣaʿb ibn ʿAlī ibn Bakr ibn Wāʾil.

The Azdī woman bore him ﷺ—a second time, through Ghālib ibn Fihr. The mother of Ghālib ibn Fihr was Laylā bt. al-Ḥārith ibn Tamīm ibn Saʿd ibn Ibn Hudhayl ibn Mudrika. Her mother was Sulaymā bt. Ṭābikha ibn Ilyās ibn Muḍar. Her mother was ʿĀtika bt. al-Azd ibn al-Ghawth.

The Fāṭimas who bore him ﷺ

One Qurashī, two Qaysī, and two Yemeni women.

As for the Qurashī woman, she bore him through his father ʿAbd Allāh. His mother was Fāṭima bt. ʿAmr ibn ʿĀʾidh ibn ʿImrān ibn Makhzūm.

The mother of ʿAmr ibn ʿĀʾidh was Fāṭima bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rizām ibn Rabīʿa ibn Jaḥūsh ibn Muʿāwiya ibn Bakr ibn Hawāzin ibn Manṣūr ibn ʿIkrima ibn Khaṣafa ibn Qays ibn ʿAylān ibn Muḍar. Her mother was Fāṭima bt. al-Ḥārith ibn Bahtha ibn Sulaym ibn Manṣūr ibn ʿIkrima. These two were the Qaysī women.

As for the two Yemeni women: the mother of Quṣayy ibn Kilāb was Fāṭima bt. Saʿd ibn Sayl—that is, Khayr ibn Ḥamāla—of the Jādira branch of Azd Shanuʾa. The mother of [the sons of Quṣayy] was Ḥubbā bt. Ḥulayl ibn Ḥabashiyya ibn Kaʿb ibn Salūl al-Khuzāʿī. [The mother of Ḥubbā] was Fāṭima bt. Naṣr ibn ʿAwf ibn ʿAmr ibn Rabīʿa ibn Ḥāritha, of Khuzāʿa.

The daughters of the Messenger of God ﷺ—their husbands, and the husbands of the caliphs

The first of his children to be born ﷺ—was Zaynab, then al-Qāsim, then Umm Kulthūm, then Fāṭima, then Ruqayya, then ʿAbd Allāh—also known as al-Ṭayyib and al-Ṭāhir—then Ibrāhīm.

Abū al-ʿĀṣ ibn al-Rabīʿ ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn ʿAbd Shams married Zaynab before Islam. She bore him ʿAlī and Umāma. ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵁ married Umāma after her maternal aunt Fāṭima, may God’s blessings be upon her. When Islam came, it separated Abū al-ʿĀṣ and Zaynab. When Abū al-ʿĀṣ became Muslim, the Prophet ﷺ—restored her to him under their original marriage contract.

ʿUtba ibn Abī Lahab married Ruqayya. Umm Jamīl bt. Ḥarb ibn Umayya ordered him to divorce her, and he did so. ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān ﵀ then married her. She bore him ʿAbd Allāh, after whom he was given his teknonym; the child died young.

ʿUthmān also married Umm Kulthūm, daughter of the Messenger of God ﷺ—after her sister Ruqayya. She bore him no children. ʿUthmān had migrated with Ruqayya to Abyssinia.

ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵁ married Fāṭima, may God’s blessings be upon her. She bore him al-Ḥasan, al-Ḥusayn, Zaynab, and Umm Kulthūm. Zaynab married ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵀; Umm Kulthūm married ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb ﵀. ʿAwn ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib later married her. Al-Madāʾinī said: Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar married her, and then ʿAwn ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar.

The sons-in-law of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq ﵀

(Muḥammad) the Messenger of God ﷺ—was married to ʿĀʾisha, may God have mercy on her.

(Al-Zubayr) ibn al-ʿAwwām was married to Asmāʾ bt. Abū Bakr.

(Ṭalḥa) ibn ʿUbayd Allāh was married to Umm Kulthūm bt. Abū Bakr.

(ʿAbd al-Raḥmān) al-Aḥwal ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn [Abī] Rabīʿa ibn al-Mughīra ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn Makhzūm married Umm Kulthūm after Ṭalḥa.

The sons-in-law of ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb ﵀

(Muḥammad) the Messenger of God ﷺ—was married to Ḥafṣa.

(Khunays) ibn Ḥudhāfa ibn Qays ibn ʿAdī ibn Saʿd ibn Sahm had been married to Ḥafṣa before the Prophet ﷺ.

(Ibrāhīm) ibn Nuʿaym al-Naḥḥām al-ʿAdawī was married to Ruqayya bt. ʿUmar. Her mother was Umm Kulthūm bt. ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵁.

The sons-in-law of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān ﵁

(ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Hishām ibn al-Mughīra) was married to Maryam bt. ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān.

(ʿAbd al-Malik) ibn Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam later married her.

Some people have disputed this. (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Khālid ibn Asīd ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ ibn Umayya was married to Umm ʿUthmān bt. ʿUthmān. (Al-Ḥārith) ibn al-Ḥakam ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ ibn Umayya was married to ʿĀʾisha bt. ʿUthmān. (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām later married her. (Marwān) ibn al-Ḥakam/ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ was married to Umm Abān bt. ʿUthmān. (Saʿīd) ibn al-ʿĀṣ ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ ibn Umayya was married to Umm ʿAmr bt. ʿUthmān. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd became his son-in-law a second time: he married Umm Khālid bt. ʿUthmān after her sister Umm ʿUthmān.

(Khālid) ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿUqba ibn Abī Muʿayṭ was married to Arwā bt. ʿUthmān.

(Abū Sufyān) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd was married to Umm al-Banīn bt. ʿUthmān.

The sons-in-law of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵁

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵀ was married to Zaynab bt. ʿAlī.

(ʿUmar) ibn al-Khaṭṭāb ﵀ was married to Umm Kulthūm bt. ʿAlī. ʿAwn, then Muḥammad, then ʿAbd Allāh—the sons of Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵀—married her in succession.

(Muslim) ibn ʿAqīl ibn Abī Ṭālib was married to Ruqayya bt. ʿAlī.

(Jaʿda) ibn Hubayra ibn Abī Wahb al-Makhzūmī was married to Umm al-Ḥasan bt. ʿAlī.

(Jaʿfar) ibn ʿAqīl later married her.

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām later married her. (Abū al-Hayyāj), namely ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, was married to Ramla bt. ʿAlī.

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn ʿAqīl ibn Abī Ṭālib was married to Umm Hānīʾ bt. ʿAlī.

(ʿAbd Allāh) also married Maymūna bt. ʿAlī.

(Tammām) ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib married Maymūna after ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAqīl.

(Firās) ibn Jaʿda ibn Hubayra was married to Zaynab al-Ṣughrā bt. ʿAlī.

(Muslim) ibn ʿAqīl became his son-in-law a second time: he married Ruqayya al-Ṣughrā bt. ʿAlī.

(Muḥammad) ibn ʿAqīl later married Ruqayya al-Ṣughrā.

(Kathīr) ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib was married to Zaynab al-Kubrā bt. ʿAlī.

(Kathīr) also married her sister, Umm Kulthūm al-Ṣughrā bt. ʿAlī.

(Muḥammad) ibn Abī Saʿīd ibn ʿAqīl was married to Fāṭima bt. ʿAlī.

(Saʿīd) ibn al-Aswad ibn Abī al-Bakhtarī was married to Fāṭima bt. ʿAlī/after (Muḥammad) ibn Abī Saʿīd.

(Al-Mundhir) ibn ʿUbayd ibn al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām married Fāṭima bt. ʿAlī after Saʿīd ibn al-Aswad.

(Al-Ṣalt) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Nawfal was married to Umāma bt. ʿAlī.

(ʿAbd al-Raḥmān) ibn ʿAqīl was married to Khadīja bt. ʿAlī.

(Abū al-Sanābil) ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Kurayz later married this Khadīja.

The sons-in-law of al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ﵁

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām was married to Umm al-Ḥasan bt. al-Ḥasan.

(ʿAmr) ibn al-Mundhir ibn al-Zubayr was married to Umm Salama bt. al-Ḥasan.

(ʿAlī) ibn al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī was married to Umm ʿAbd Allāh bt. al-Ḥasan.

The sons-in-law of Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn ʿĀmir ibn Kurayz ibn Rabīʿa ibn Ḥabīb ibn ʿAbd Shams was married to Hind bt. Muʿāwiya.

(ʿAmr) ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān was married to Ramla bt. Muʿāwiya.

(ʿAmh) ibn Ziyād ibn Abīhi3 was married to Ṣafiyya bt. Muʿāwiya.

The sons-in-law of Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya

(ʿAbd al-Malik) ibn Marwān was married to ʿĀtika bt. Yazīd.

(Al-Aṣbagh) ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān was married to Umm Yazīd bt. Yazīd.

(ʿAmr) ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān was married to Umm Muḥammad bt. Yazīd.

(ʿAbbād) ibn Ziyād ibn Abīhi was married to Umm ʿAbd al-Raḥmān bt. Yazīd.

(ʿUthmān) ibn Muḥammad ibn Abī Sufyān was married to Umm ʿUthmān bt. Yazīd.

(ʿUtba) ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān was married to Ramla bt. Yazīd. After ʿUtba ibn ʿUtba, (ʿAbbād) ibn Ziyād ibn Abīhi married Ramla.

Muʿāwiya ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya left no descendants; his line died out.

The sons-in-law of Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam

(Al-Walīd) ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān was married to Umm ʿAmr bt. Marwān.

(ʿAbd al-Malik) ibn al-Ḥārith ibn al-Ḥakam was married to Umm ʿUthmān bt. Marwān.

(Abū Bakr) ibn al-Ḥārith ibn al-Ḥakam was married to Ramla bt. Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam.

The sons-in-law of ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām ibn Khuwaylid was married to Fāṭima bt. ʿAbd Allāh.

(Khālid) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ later married her.

(Muḥammad) ibn al-Mundhir ibn al-Zubayr was married to Fākhita bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr.

The sons-in-law of ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān

(Khālid) ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān was married to ʿĀʾisha bt. ʿAbd al-Malik.

(ʿUmar) ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz was married to Fāṭima bt. ʿAbd al-Malik.

Neither al-Walīd nor Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik had daughters.

The sons-in-law of ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān

(Sufyān) ibn ʿĀṣim ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān was married to Amat Allāh bt. ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz.

(Ḥassān) ibn al-Aṣbagh ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān was married to Umm ʿAbd Allāh bt. ʿUmar.

The sons-in-law of Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān

(Muḥammad) ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān was married to ʿĀtika bt. Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

The sons-in-law of Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Marwān was married to Umm Hishām bt. Hishām.

(ʿUbayd Allāh) ibn Marwān ibn Muḥammad ibn Marwān was married to ʿĀʾisha bt. Hishām.

(Muḥammad) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān was married to Zaynab bt. Hishām.

(ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz) ibn al-Ḥajjāj ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān was married to Umm Salama bt. Hishām.

Neither al-Walīd ibn Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik, nor Yazīd nor Ibrāhīm, the sons of al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik, had daughters.

The sons-in-law of Marwān ibn Muḥammad ibn Marwān

(Muḥammad) ibn Manṣūr ibn Muḥammad ibn Marwān was married to Umm Marwān bt. Marwān ibn Muḥammad.

(Al-Walīd) ibn Muʿāwiya ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān was married to Umm al-Walīd bt. Marwān.

(Abān) ibn Yazīd ibn Muḥammad ibn Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam was married to Umm ʿUthmān bt. Marwān.

The sons-in-law of the caliphs from Banū Hāshim

The sons-in-law of the Commander of the Faithful, Abū al-ʿAbbās

(Muḥammad) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās had married Rayṭa bt. Abū al-ʿAbbās, but he died before consummating the marriage.

(Al-Mahdī) ibn al-Manṣūr later married her.

The sons-in-law of al-Manṣūr

(Isḥāq) ibn ʿĪsā ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās married al-ʿĀliya bt. al-Manṣūr.

The Sons-in-law of al-Mahdī

(Muḥammad) ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās was married to al-ʿAbbāsa, daughter of al-Mahdī; she was widowed by his death.

(Ibrāhīm) ibn Ṣāliḥ ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās then married her.

(Ibrāhīm) ibn Jaʿfar the Elder ibn al-Manṣūr was married to Umm ʿAbd Allāh, daughter of al-Mahdī, and she bore him Umm Mūsā.

(Mūsā) ibn ʿĪsā ibn Mūsā was married to ʿAliyya, daughter of al-Mahdī, and she bore him ʿĪsā and Asmāʾ.

The Sons-in-law of al-Hādī

(ʿAbd Allāh) al-Maʾmūn was married to Umm ʿĪsā, daughter of Mūsā.

The Sons-in-law of al-Rashīd

(Muḥammad) ibn Ṣāliḥ ibn al-Manṣūr was married to Khadīja, daughter of al-Rashīd.

(Jaʿfar) ibn al-Hādī was married to Umm Muḥammad, daughter of al-Rashīd, namely Ḥamdūna, daughter of ʿUḍayḍ.

The Sons-in-law of al-Maʾmūn

(Muḥammad) ibn al-Muʿtaṣim was married to ʿĀʾisha, daughter of al-Maʾmūn.

(Al-Wāthiq) Hārūn ibn al-Muʿtaṣim married Asmāʾ, daughter of al-Maʾmūn, but he did not consummate the marriage.

(Al-Mutawakkil) ibn al-Muʿtaṣim was married to Nāmiyya, daughter of al-Maʾmūn; she died while married to him.

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Manṣūr ibn al-Mahdī was married to Amīna, daughter of al-Maʾmūn.

(Muḥammad) ibn ʿAlī ibn Mūsā ibn Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib was married to Umm al-Faḍl, daughter of al-Maʾmūn.

The Sons-in-law of al-Muʿtaṣim

(Al-ʿAbbās) ibn al-Maʾmūn married Umm Muḥammad, daughter of al-Muʿtaṣim, but did not consummate the marriage.

The Sons-in-law of al-Wāthiq

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Muʿtaṣim married Fāṭima, daughter of al-Wāthiq.

(Aḥmad) ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Muʿtaṣim married ʿĀʾisha, daughter of al-Wāthiq.

The Sons-in-law of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib ibn Hāshim and of the Notable Men among His Sons

(Kurayz) ibn Rabīʿa ibn Ḥabīb ibn ʿAbd Shams was married to Umm Ḥakīm, namely al-Bayḍāʾ, daughter of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

(Abū Umayya) ibn al-Mughīra ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn Makhzūm was married to ʿĀtika, daughter of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

(ʿAbd al-Asad) ibn Hilāl ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn Makhzūm was married to Barra, daughter of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

(Abū Ruhm) ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn Abī Qays ibn ʿAbd Wudd ibn Naṣr ibn Mālik ibn Ḥisl ibn ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy married Barra after ʿAbd al-Asad.

(Jaḥsh) ibn Riʾāb ibn Yaʿmar ibn Ṣabra ibn Murra ibn Kabīr ibn Ghanm ibn Dūdān ibn Asad ibn Khuzayma was married to Umayma, daughter of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

(ʿUmayr) ibn Wahb ibn ʿAbd ibn Quṣayy was married to Ṣafiyya, daughter of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

(Al-ʿAwwām) ibn Khuwaylid ibn Asad ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā married Ṣafiyya after ʿUmayr ibn Wahb.

The Sons-in-law of al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib

(Al-Aswad) ibn Sufyān ibn ʿAbd al-Asad ibn Hilāl ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn Makhzūm was married to Umm Ḥabīb, daughter of al-ʿAbbās.

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Abī Masrūḥ, whose name was al-Ḥārith ibn Yaʿmar ibn Ḥibbān ibn ʿUmayra ibn Mallān ibn Nāṣira ibn Quṣayya ibn Naṣr ibn Saʿd ibn Bakr ibn Hawāzin, was married to Ṣafiyya, daughter of al-ʿAbbās.

(Al-ʿAbbās) ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Lahab was married to Umayma, daughter of al-ʿAbbās.

The Sons-in-law of Abū Ṭālib ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib

(Hubayra) ibn Abī Wahb ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿĀʾidh ibn ʿImrān ibn Makhzūm, was married to Hind, namely Umm Hāniʾ, daughter of Abū Ṭālib.

(Abū Sufyān) ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib was married to al-Jumāna, daughter of Abū Ṭālib.

The Sons-in-law of al-Zubayr ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib

(Rabīʿa) ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib was married to Umm al-Ḥakam, daughter of al-Zubayr ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

(Al-Miqdād) ibn ʿAmr al-Bahrānī, ally of Banū Zuhra, who is attributed to al-Aswad, was married to Ḍubāʿa, daughter of al-Zubayr.

(ʿAbd al-Raḥmān) ibn al-Aswad ibn ʿAbd Yaghūth al-Zuhrī married Ḍubāʿa after al-Miqdād.

The Sons-in-law of Ḥamza ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib

(Salama) ibn Abī Salama al-Makhzūmī—and the Messenger of God ﷺ had married him to Umāma, daughter of Ḥamza ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib—she died before he consummated the marriage.

The Sons-in-law of al-Muqawwim ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib

(Abū ʿAmra) ibn ʿAmr ibn Miḥṣan ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAtīk, of Banū ʿAmr ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Mālik ibn al-Najjār, among the Anṣār, was married to Hind, daughter of al-Muqawwim. (Abū Masrūḥ), namely al-Ḥārith ibn Yaʿmar ibn Ḥibbān ibn ʿUmayra ibn Mallān ibn Nāḍira ibn Quṣayya ibn Saʿd ibn Bakr ibn Hawāzin, was married to Arwā, daughter of al-Muqawwim.

(Masʿūd) ibn Muʿattib al-Thaqafī was married to Umm ʿAmr, daughter of al-Muqawwim, whose name was Fākhta.

(Muʿattib) ibn Abī Lahab ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib married Fākhta after Masʿūd.

(Abū Sufyān) ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib married Fākhta after Muʿattib ibn Abī Lahab.

The Sons-in-law of Abū Lahab ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib

(Al-Ḥārith) ibn ʿĀmir ibn Nawfal ibn ʿAbd Manāf was married to Durra, daughter of Abū Lahab.

(Diḥya) ibn Khalīfa al-Kalbī married Durra after al-Ḥārith.

(Awfā) ibn Ḥakīm ibn Umayya ibn Ḥāritha ibn al-Awqaṣ al-Sulamī was married to ʿAzza, daughter of Abū Lahab.

(ʿUthmān) ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ ibn Bishr ibn ʿAbd Dahmān al-Thaqafī was married to Khālida, daughter of Abū Lahab.

The Sons-in-law of al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib

(Abū Wadāʿa) ibn Ḍubayra al-Sahmī was married to Arwā, daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

These are the sons-in-law of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib and of the notable men among his sons.

The Sons-in-law of the Members of the Consultative Council

They are Ṭalḥa, al-Zubayr, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf, Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ, and Saʿīd ibn Zayd:

The Sons-in-law of Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq ﵁ was married to ʿĀʾisha, daughter of Ṭalḥa.

(Muṣʿab) ibn al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām married ʿĀʾisha after ʿAbd Allāh.

(ʿUmar) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Maʿmar al-Taymī married ʿĀʾisha after Muṣʿab.

(Al-Ḥasan) ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵁ was married to Umm Isḥāq, daughter of Ṭalḥa.

(Al-Ḥusayn) ibn ʿAlī ﵁ married Umm Isḥāq after al-Ḥasan.

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq ﵁ married Umm Isḥāq after al-Ḥusayn.

(Tammām) ibn al-Mughīra ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Maʿmar ibn ʿUthmān al-Taymī was married to al-Ṣaʿba, daughter of Ṭalḥa.

(ʿAnbasa) ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ married al-Ṣaʿba, daughter of Ṭalḥa, after Tammām ibn al-Mughīra.

(ʿUmar) ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAmr ibn Kaʿb ibn Saʿd ibn Taym was married to Maryam, daughter of Ṭalḥa.

The Sons-in-law of al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Abī Rabīʿa ibn al-Mughīra ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn Makhzūm was married to Khadīja the Elder, daughter of al-Zubayr. Then (Jubayr) ibn Muṭʿim ibn ʿAdī ibn Nawfal ibn ʿAbd Manāf married Khadīja the Elder.

Then (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Abī Rabīʿa again. Then (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn al-Sāʾib ibn Abī Ḥubaysh ibn al-Muṭṭalib ibn Asad married Khadīja after ʿAbd Allāh. (ʿAbd al-Raḥmān) ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām was married to Umm al-Ḥasan, daughter of al-Zubayr. (Al-Walīd) ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān was married to ʿĀʾisha, daughter of al-Zubayr. (Yaʿlā) ibn Munya al-Tamīmī was married to Ḥabība, daughter of al-Zubayr. (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAlqama married Ḥabība. (ʿAmr) ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ was married to Sawda, daughter of al-Zubayr. (ʿAbd al-Malik) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Kurayz was married to Hind, daughter of al-Zubayr. (ʿAbbās) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib married Hind. (ʿUthmān) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām ibn Khuwaylid was married to Ramla, daughter of al-Zubayr. (Khālid) ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān married Ramla. (Abū Yasār) ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Shayba ibn Rabīʿa ibn ʿAbd Shams was married to Khadīja the Younger, daughter of al-Zubayr.

The Sons-in-law of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf

(Yazīd) ibn Abī Sufyān ibn Ḥarb was married to Fākhta, daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān.

(Yaḥyā) ibn al-Ḥakam ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ ibn Umayya was married to Umm al-Qāsim the Younger, daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān. It is said instead that (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān married her.

(ʿAbd Allāh) the Elder ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān was married to Umm al-Ḥakam, daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān.

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn al-Aswad ibn ʿAwf was married to Ḥamīda, daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān.

(Abū ʿUbayda) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAwf was married to Amat al-Raḥmān, daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān.

(ʿAbd Allāh) ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib was married to Ṣaʿba, daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān.

(Ibrāhīm) ibn Qāriẓ ibn Khālid al-Kinānī was married to Āmina, daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, and he also married Maryam, daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, after her sister Āmina.

(Al-Miswar) ibn Makhrama was married to Juwayriyya, daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān.

(ʿUmar) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAwf was married to Umm Yaḥyā, daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān.

The Sons-in-law of Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ

(Al-Mughīra) ibn Shuʿba ibn Abī ʿĀmir ibn Masʿūd ibn Muʿattib al-Thaqafī was married to Ḥafṣa, daughter of Saʿd.

(Muʿāwiya) ibn ʿUmayr ibn Isḥāq ibn Muʿāwiya al-Kindī married Ḥafṣa.
