He was Daniel ibn Yaḥannā ibn Ḥizqiyā, who was Yūnākhīn, son of King Ṣidqiyā ibn Aḥiyāqīm ibn Ushiyā ibn Amīn ibn Ḥizqiyā ibn Aḥādhīn ibn Yāthim ibn ʿAzariyā ibn Amāṣiyā ibn Muhiyās ibn Akhziyā ibn Rabhiyā ibn Rām ibn Yāhūshā ibn Āsā ibn Abiyā ibn Rāḥabʿām ibn Solomon ibn David.

The names of those concerning whom God revealed, “When they meet those who believe, they say, ‘We believe,’ but when they are alone with their devils, they say, ‘We are with you’”: they were five Jewish priests. No one can be a priest without having a devil who follows him. They were (Kaʿb) ibn al-Ashraf, a Ṭāʾī, in Medina;

(Ḥayy) ibn Akhṭab, in Medina;

(Abū Bardah) al-Aslamī, of Khuzāʿah;

(ibn al-Sawdāʾ), of Banū al-Ḥārith ibn Saʿd Hudhaym, in Syria. He was the grandfather of the poet Hudbah ibn Khashram. And (ʿAbd al-Dār) ibn Ḥudayb, of Juhaynah.

And ʿAwf ibn ʿĀmir, among Banū Asad ibn Khuzayma. He practiced soothsaying in the days of Ḥujr, Abū Imruʾ al-Qays.

Names of things mentioned by God—Mighty and Glorious—in His Book

/ The name of the Samaritan’s calf was Buhayūthā. The name of Abraham’s ram ﵇ was Jarīr. The name of the man who said to Moses, “Indeed, the chiefs are conspiring against you” 28:20, was Shimʿūn. The name of the jinn who said to Solomon ﵇ “I shall bring it to you before you rise from your place” 27:39, was Kawdan.

The names of the two messengers concerning whom God said, “When We sent two to them, they rejected them, so We strengthened them with a third” 36:14: before Jesus ﵇ was raised up, he sent three messengers to the people of Antioch. The two were Yūḥannā and Shimʿūn; the third was Yūlīs Ṭuwā or Būlus Ṭuwā. The name of the man who said, “Burn him and support your gods” 21:68, was Hīran. God—Mighty and Glorious—caused the earth to swallow him, and he will continue to sink into it until the Day of Resurrection. The name of the man who used to “seize every” 18:79 “ship by force” 18:79 was Hadad ibn Baddad. The name of the one whom al-Khiḍr killed was Ḥaysūr or Jaysūr. Ibn al-Kalbī says it was Khushnūdh. The name of the one who said to Solomon ﵇ “I shall bring it to you before your glance returns to you” 27:40, and of whom it was said, “he had knowledge of the Book” 27:40, was Āṣif ibn Barkhiyā ibn Shamʿiyā. His name was Nāṭūrā.

Names of those who ruled the whole earth, among jinn and humans, according to Ibn al-Kalbī

The first of the jinn’s descendants to rule it was Jayūmart. Then his son Ṭahmūrt ruled it, and after him it was ruled by Ūshīnk, who is also Awsahanj.

Then God—Mighty and Glorious—created Adam, blessings be upon him, in the time of Ūshīnk. Some of the Magians say that he was Adam ibn Ūshīnk, though not all of them say so.

The first of Adam’s descendants to rule it was Jam Shādh ibn Yūwanjahān, of the descendants of Cain. Every day he would traverse the world as the sun traverses it:

He would rise at dawn in the east and set at evening in the west. He ruled it when the people numbered one hundred souls, in the time between Noah and Adam ﵉.

The second to rule it after him was Nimrod ibn Canaan ibn Ham ibn Noah, Abraham’s contemporary. He ruled it when the people numbered ten thousand souls.

The third was al-Baywarāsb, that is, al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays, the two-serpent bearer, Satan’s friend. Satan kissed his back, whereupon two serpents appeared on his shoulders. He ruled the world for a thousand years. Hishām ibn al-Kalbī said: “My father, ʿAwāna, and Sharqī used to say: al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn al-Ahyūb ibn al-Azd ibn al-Ghawth.”

The Persians claim him. Then Solomon ibn David ﵉, ruled it, followed by Dhū al-Qarnayn, who was Hermes ibn Mayṭūn ibn Rūmī ibn Lanṭī ibn Kislūḥīn ibn Yūnān ibn Yāfith ibn Noah.

Ibn al-Kalbī also said: “Four men ruled the earth, two righteous and two wicked. The righteous were Solomon ibn David and Dhū al-Qarnayn. The wicked were Nimrod ibn Canaan, Abraham’s contemporary, and Bukht Naṣṣar, whose name was Banūkhadh Naṣṣar ibn Sanḥārīb ibn Kayḥasrī, one of the early Persians, before the Nabataeans and before the Banū Sāsān. The early Persians were called the Ashkanān, and the Nabataeans the Ardwān.

They were the kings of the regional dynasties. The later Persians were the Banū Sāsān. Sāsān was a servant of Bābakān.

Hishām said on another occasion: “The two wicked men were Nimrod and al-Ḍaḥḥāk.”

The sons of Abraham ﵇

Ishmael, whose mother was Hājar, a concubine; Isaac, whose mother was Sarah daughter of Lābin ibn Bathūʾīl; Midian; Madūn;

Yaqshān; Zamrān; Ashbaq; and Shaḥ. Their mother was Qanṭūrā daughter of Maqṭūr, one of the Arabized peoples. He sent Madūn, Ashbaq, and Shaḥ ﷺ and kept the rest. These three came to Khurāsān, where their descendants multiplied.

Khurāsān was left to them.

Tribes of the Arabized peoples who were inspired with Arabic and spoke it

ʿĀd and ʿAbīl, sons of ʿAwṣ ibn Iram ibn Sām ibn Noah.

Thamūd and Jadīs, sons of Jāthir ibn Iram ibn Sām ibn Noah. ʿAmālīq, Ṭasm, and Umaym, sons of Lūdhān ibn Iram ibn Sām ibn Noah. The Banū Yaqṭan, descendants of ʿĀbir ibn Shālikh ibn Arfakhshadh ibn Sām ibn Noah, namely Jurhum, Ḥaḍramawt, and al-Salaf. Jāsim ibn ʿAmmān ibn Sabaʾ ibn Yaqshān ibn Abraham ﵇.

Names of the descendants of Satan, may God curse him

Isḥāq ibn Ismāʿīl al-Ṭāliqānī related from Jarīr, from Layth, from Mujāhid, who said: “Satan had five children, among whom he divided evil. They were al-Thabr, Zulafiyūn, and Dāmis.” Al-Ziyādī said: “They were al-Thabr, Dāsim, al-Aʿwar, and Masūṭ.” Al-Thabr is the patron of calamities. Zulafiyūn is the one who incites people against one another. Dāmis is the patron of whisperings. Al-Aʿwar is the patron of fornication.

Masūṭ is the bearer of the banner, which he plants in the middle of the market. He goes out with the first to go out, setting disputes and quarrels among the people.

Women who remained faithful to their husbands and did not marry after them

Umm Hāniʾ daughter of Abū Ṭālib. The Messenger of God ﷺ proposed to her.

She had been married to Hubayra ibn Abī Wahb al-Makhzūmī. When Mecca was conquered, he fled from the Messenger of God ﷺ and died an unbeliever in Yemen. She said to the Prophet ﷺ: “By God, I loved you in the pre-Islamic period; how much more so in Islam! But I am a woman with children, and I fear they may cause you trouble.” He ﷺ said: “The best women to ride camels are the women of Quraysh: the most tender toward a child in its youth, and the most careful with a husband’s property.” And Nāʾila daughter of al-Furāfiṣa al-Kalbiyya, the wife of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān ﵀.

Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān proposed to her and pressed her insistently. She pulled out her two front teeth and sent them to him. At that point, he desisted from pursuing her.

And al-Rubāb daughter of Imruʾ al-Qays ibn ʿAdī ibn Jābir ibn Kaʿb ibn ʿUlaym. Al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ﵄ said of her:

By your life! I love a house

Where Sukayna and al-Rubāb dwell.

She was married to al-Ḥusayn ﵁. When he was killed, she was proposed to. She said: “By God, I shall not take another father-in-law after the Messenger of God ﷺ.” And Umm al-Dardāʾ, the wife of Abū al-Dardāʾ. Muʿāwiya proposed to her. She said:

“I would never choose anyone over Abū al-Dardāʾ. The Messenger of God ﷺ said: ‘When a woman and her two husbands are together in Paradise, she will belong to the last of them.’” And the wife of Hudba ibn Khushrum al-ʿUdhrī. When he was brought forward to be executed in retaliation, he raised his head and said:

Do not marry, if fate should part us,

A man with a nape and face both smooth and unmarked,

Who strikes his two jaws against the bone of his chest,

And, when the people resolve to act, covers himself.

She asked the people to delay Hudba for a little while. Then she went to a butcher and took a knife from him, with which she mutilated her nose. She came to him before he was killed, her nose mutilated, and said: “Do you see a woman with a husband?”

Women who, when they spent the morning with a husband, had the choice in the matter: they could stay if they wished or leave if they wished, on account of their nobility and rank

Salmā daughter of ʿAmr ibn Zayd ibn Lubayd ibn Khudāsh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Ghanm ibn ʿAdī ibn al-Najjār, who was Taym Allāh ibn Thaʿlaba ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Khazraj.

She was the mother of ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib ibn Hāshim ibn ʿAbd Manāf.

Fāṭima daughter of al-Kharshab al-Anmāriyya. She was the mother of the “perfect ones” among Banū ʿAbs: al-Rabīʿ al-Kāmil, ʿImāra al-Wahhāb, Qays al-Ḥifāẓ, and Anas al-Fawāris, sons of Ziyād.

Umm Khārija, namely ʿAmra daughter of Saʿd ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qudād ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Muʿāwiya ibn Zayd ibn al-Ghawth ibn Anmār, of Bajīla. She had many children among the Arabs, and the saying “quicker than Umm Khārija’s marriage” is applied to her.

Māriya daughter of al-Juʿayd ibn Ḍabra ibn al-Dayl ibn Shann ibn Afṣā ibn ʿAbd al-Qays ibn Afṣā ibn Lakīz. She, too, had many children among the Arabs.

ʿĀtika daughter of Murra ibn Hilāl ibn Fālij ibn Dhakwān ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Buhtha ibn Sulaym ibn Manṣūr. She was the mother of Hāshim, ʿAbd Shams, and al-Muṭṭalib, sons of ʿAbd Manāf.

Al-Sawāʾ daughter of al-Aʿyas, of ʿAnaza and then Banū Hizzān.

She was married to Khālid ibn Jaʿfar ibn Kilāb.

A woman whose father, grandfather, and husband fought at Badr

Umm Kulthūm daughter of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵁. Her father was ʿAlī, her grandfather was the Messenger of God ﷺ and her husband was ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb ﵀.

A woman whose seven sons fought alongside the Messenger of God ﷺ

ʿAfrāʾ daughter of ʿUbayd ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Ghanm ibn Mālik ibn al-Najjār. She was married to al-Ḥārith ibn Rifāʿa ibn Sawād ibn Mālik ibn Ghanm ibn al-Najjār. She bore him Muʿādh and Muʿawwidh. Then he divorced her. She came to Mecca and married Bukayr ibn ʿAbd Yālīl ibn Nāshib ibn Ghayra ibn Saʿd ibn Layth ibn Bakr ibn ʿAbd Manāt ibn Kināna. She bore him Khālid, Iyās, ʿĀqil, and ʿĀmir, sons of Bakr [perhaps Bukayr]. Then she returned to Medina, and al-Ḥārith ibn Rifāʿa took her back. She bore him ʿAwf. They all fought at Badr. Muʿādh, Muʿawwidh, and ʿĀqil were martyred on the day of Badr; Khālid on the day of al-Rajīʿ; ʿĀmir on the day of Biʾr Maʿūna; and Iyās on the day of al-Yamāma. The remaining one of them was ʿAwf.

A woman whose two brothers fought at Badr with the Messenger of God ﷺ and whose two brothers fought at Badr with the polytheists; whose paternal uncle fought at Badr with the Messenger of God ﷺ and whose paternal uncle fought at Badr with the polytheists; and whose maternal uncle fought at Badr with the Prophet ﷺ and whose maternal uncle fought at Badr with the polytheists

Umm Abān daughter of ʿUtba ibn Rabīʿa ibn ʿAbd Shams. One of her two brothers was Abū Ḥudhayfa ibn ʿUtba; the other, her maternal brother, was Muṣʿab al-Khayr ibn ʿUmayr ibn Hāshim ibn ʿAbd Manāf ibn ʿAbd al-Dār. He fought at Badr with the Messenger of God ﷺ. Her mother was Khunās daughter of Mālik ibn Muḍarrib—whose name was Wahb—ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥujayr ibn ʿAbd ibn Maʿīṣ ibn ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy. One of her two brothers was with the polytheists— (al-Walīd) ibn ʿUtbah, and the other through her mother, (Abū ʿAzīz), who was Zurarah ibn ʿUmayr ibn Hāshim.

One of her paternal uncles was with the Messenger of God ﷺ: (Muʿammar) ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Muʿammar ibn Ḥabīb ibn Wahb ibn Ḥudhāfah ibn Jumāḥ. He was ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah’s maternal half-brother: their mother was Hind bt. al-Muḍrib. Her other paternal uncle was with the polytheists: (Shaybah) ibn Rabīʿah ibn ʿAbd Shams.

One of her maternal uncles was with the Messenger of God ﷺ: (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Suhayl ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd Shams ibn ʿAbd Wadd ibn Naṣr ibn Mālik ibn Ḥisl ibn ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy. Her other maternal uncle was with the polytheists: (Abū Khunās), who was Shaybah ibn Mālik ibn Muḍrib.

Her father, (ʿUtbah) ibn Abī Sufyān, was with the polytheists.

A woman whose father and paternal uncle fought at Badr with the Prophet ﷺ while her two maternal uncles fought with the polytheists

ʿĀtikah bt. Saʿīd ibn Zayd ibn ʿAmr ibn Nufayl. Her father (Saʿīd), and her paternal uncle (ʿUmārah) ibn Ḥazm al-Anṣārī were with the Messenger of God ﷺ. He was Saʿīd’s maternal half-brother: their mother was Fāṭimah bt. Baʿjah ibn Umayyah ibn Khuwaylid, from Khuzāʿah. Her maternal uncles were (al-Ḥārith and ʿĀmir), the sons of al-Ḥaḍramī. They were Ṭalḥah ibn ʿUbayd Allāh’s maternal uncles. They were killed as unbelievers.

A woman whose father and paternal uncle fought at Badr with the Messenger of God ﷺ; whose other paternal uncle fought with the polytheists; and whose one maternal uncle was with the Prophet while the other was with the polytheists

Zaynab bt. Abī Salamah ibn ʿAbd al-Asad. Her father was (Abū Salamah), and her paternal uncle was (Abū Sabrah) ibn Abī Rahm, Abū Salamah’s maternal half-brother. Her other paternal uncle was with the polytheists: (al-Aswad) ibn ʿAbd al-Asad. Her maternal uncle (Ḥamzah) ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib was with the Messenger of God ﷺ. Her other maternal uncle was with the polytheists: (Masʿūd) ibn Abī Umayyah ibn al-Mughīrah.

A woman whose brother, maternal uncle, and husband were killed alongside the Messenger of God ﷺ at Uḥud

Ḥamnah bt. Jaḥsh. Her brother was (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Jaḥsh, and her maternal uncle was (Ḥamzah) ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, and her husband was (Muṣʿab) al-Khayr ibn ʿUmayr.

A woman whose four husbands and brother fought at Badr with the Prophet ﷺ

Jamīlah bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl. Her husband was (Mālik) ibn al-Dukhshum ibn Marḍakhah ibn Ghanm, who was Qawqal. (Ḥanẓalah) ibn Abī ʿĀmir al-Ghasīl was killed at Uḥud. (Khubaib) ibn Isāf was killed on the day of al-Yamāmah.

And (Thābit) ibn Qays ibn Shammās was killed on the day of al-Yamāmah. Her brother was (al-Ḥubāb).

The Prophet ﷺ named him “ʿAbd Allāh.” He was ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl. She was the mother of Abū Mālik ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿUbayd, who was killed as a martyr on the day of al-Yamāmah.

A woman whose two husbands, son, and brother’s son fought at Badr with the Prophet ﷺ

Jamīlah bt. Abī ʿĀmir ibn Ṣayfī ibn al-Nuʿmān ibn Mālik al-Anṣārī.

Her husbands were (Zayd) ibn al-Khaṭṭāb and (Saʿd) ibn Khaythamah ibn al-Ḥārith al-Awsī.

Her son was (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Saʿd, and her brother’s son was (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Ḥanẓalah.

A woman to whom the Messenger of God, Abū Bakr, ʿUmar, ʿUthmān, ʿAlī, Ṭalḥah, and al-Zubayr each gave a child

Ḥafṣah bt. Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn (ʿUthmān). Her mother was Khadījah bt. ʿUthmān ibn ʿUrwah ibn (al-Zubayr). ʿUrwah’s mother was Asmāʾ bt. (Abū Bakr). Muḥammad’s mother—his father’s mother—was Fāṭimah bt. al-Ḥusayn ibn (ʿAlī). The mother of Fāṭimah bt. al-Ḥusayn was Umm Isḥāq bt. (Ṭalḥah) ibn ʿUbayd Allāh. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān’s mother was Ḥafṣah bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn (ʿUmar) ibn al-Khaṭṭāb. It is also said that she was Zaynab bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar.

A woman whose brother, son, and husband were killed at Uḥud alongside the Messenger of God ﷺ

Hind bt. ʿAmr ibn Ḥarām, the paternal aunt of Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh. Her brother, (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn ʿAmr, her husband, (ʿAmr) ibn al-Jamūḥ, and her son, (Khallād) ibn ʿAmr, were killed.

A woman who counts twelve caliphs, all of them unmarriageable kin to her

ʿĀtikah bt. Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiyah. Her father was (Yazīd), and her grandfather (Muʿāwiyah); her brother was (Muʿāwiyah) ibn Yazīd; her husband was (ʿAbd al-Malik) ibn Marwān; her father-in-law was (Marwān) ibn al-Ḥakam; her husband’s sons were (al-Walīd), (Sulaymān), and (Hishām); her son was (Yazīd) ibn ʿAbd al-Malik; her son’s son was (al-Walīd) ibn Yazīd; and her husband’s son’s sons were (Yazīd) and (Ibrāhīm), the sons of al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

A woman who counts nine caliphs, all of them unmarriageable kin to her

Zubaydah bt. Jaʿfar ibn al-Manṣūr. Her husband was (Hārūn al-Rashīd).

Her son was (al-Amīn). Her husband’s sons were (al-Maʾmūn) and (al-Muʿtaṣim). Her husband’s son’s sons were (al-Wāthiq) and (al-Mutawakkil). Her paternal uncle was (al-Mahdī). Her grandfather was (al-Manṣūr), and her father’s paternal uncle was (Abū al-ʿAbbās).

A woman who counts ten caliphs, all of them unmarriageable kin to her

Umm Ḥabīb bt. al-Rashīd. Her father was (al-Rashīd), and her paternal uncle was (al-Hādī).

Her grandfather was (al-Mahdī), her father’s grandfather was (al-Manṣūr), and her grandfather’s paternal uncle was (Abū al-ʿAbbās).

Her brothers were (al-Amīn), (al-Maʾmūn), and (al-Muʿtaṣim). Her brother’s sons were (al-Wāthiq) and (al-Mutawakkil). (Al-Muntaṣir) was her brother’s son’s son. And (Aḥmad) ibn Muḥammad ibn al-Muʿtaṣim, her brother’s son’s son.

Names of the women of Banū Hāshim who swore allegiance to the Messenger of God ﷺ as reported by al-Wāqidī

The daughters of the Messenger of God ﷺ: (Zaynab), (Umm Kulthūm), (Fāṭimah), and (Ruqayyah). (Ṣafiyyah) bt. ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, the mother of al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām. (Arwā) bt. ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, the mother of Ṭulayb ibn ʿUmayr. (ʿĀtikah) bt. ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, the mother of ʿAbd Allāh and Zuhayr, the sons of Abū Umayyah. (Ṣafiyyah) bt. al-Zubayr ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. And (Umm al-Zubayr) bt. al-Zubayr ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

And (Ḍubāʿah) bt. al-Zubayr ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. (Umm al-Ḥakam) bt. al-Zubayr ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. (Umm Hāniʾ) bt. Abī Ṭālib, whose name was Fākhitah. (Jumānah) bt. Abī Ṭālib. He said: ʿAbd al-Ḥakam ibn ʿAbd Allāh related this to us, from al-Aʿraj. (Durrah) bt. Abī Lahab. (Umm Ayman), whose name was Barakah; she was the mother of Usāmah ibn Zayd ibn Ḥārithah. She swore allegiance at Mecca before the Hijrah. She was the client of ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. And (Salmā), the client of the Messenger of God ﷺ; she was the wife of Abū Rāfiʿ, the Prophet’s client ﷺ.

Among Banū al-Muṭṭalib ibn ʿAbd Manāf

(Umm Ramthah) bt. ʿAmr ibn Hāshim ibn al-Muṭṭalib. She was the mother of Ḥakīm Abū al-Qaʿqāʿ ibn Ḥakīm al-Azdī, an ally of Banū al-Muṭṭalib.

(Buḥaynah) bt. al-Ḥārith, who was al-Art ibn al-Muṭṭalib. She was the mother of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Buḥaynah, who was ʿAbd Allāh ibn Mālik al-Azdī.

Among Banū Nawfal ibn ʿAbd Manāf

(Khawlah) bt. Ḥakīm ibn al-Awqaṣ al-Sulamī. She had been married to ʿUthmān ibn Maẓʿūn al-Jumaḥī. She swore allegiance at Mecca before the Hijrah.

Among Banū ʿAbd Shams

(Arwā) bt. Kurayz, the mother of ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān and al-Walīd ibn ʿUqbah.

(Umm Kulthūm) bt. ʿUqbah, who was Arwā’s daughter and the sister of al-Walīd ibn ʿUqbah.

She was among those who emigrated. Her emigration, and that of Arwā bt. Kurayz, took place after the Prophet ﷺ had emigrated to Medina. Umm Kulthūm arrived alone during the truce and swore allegiance. She had been among those who swore allegiance at Mecca before the Hijrah. Then her mother, Arwā, arrived after her. It was concerning Umm Kulthūm that this was revealed: “Test them; God knows their faith best” (60:10). Umm Kulthūm had no husband at Mecca. When she arrived, al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām married her and divorced her. Then ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf married her after him.

She bore him Ibrāhīm and Ḥumayd. Then he died, leaving her a widow. ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ then married her.

And (Umm Ḥabībah) bt. Abī Sufyān. And (Hind) bt. ʿUtbah ibn Rabīʿah. She swore allegiance on the day of the Conquest.

Among their allies

(Zaynab) bt. Jaḥsh, (Umm Ḥabībah) bt. Jaḥsh, (Ḥamnah) bt. Jaḥsh, (Judāmah) bt. Jandal al-Asadiyyah, (Umm Qays) bt. Miḥṣan, the sister of ʿUkāshah ibn Miḥṣan and Abū Sinān ibn Miḥṣan al-Asadī;

(Umm Ḥabībah) bt. Nabātah, an Asadiyyah; and (Umayyah) bt. Ruqaysh, the sister of Yazīd ibn Ruqaysh, an Asadiyyah.

Among Banū Asad ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā

(Khadījah) bt. Khuwaylid ﵂ was the first person to embrace Islam at Mecca, in the year the Messenger of God ﷺ issued his call. And (al-Ḥawl alāʾ) bt. Tuwayt swore allegiance in Medina after the Hijrah.

Among Banū ʿAbd al-Dār

(Barakah) bt. Yasār, the sister of Abū Tajrāh, a client of Banū ʿAbd al-Dār.

They say, “We are from Yemen.” She swore allegiance at Mecca and was among the earliest emigrants.

Among Banū Zuhrah

(Khālidah) bt. al-Aswad ibn ʿAbd Yaghūth. She swore allegiance at Mecca and was among the early Muslims. It is reported that the Prophet ﷺ visited ʿĀʾishah and saw with her a woman of beautiful appearance: (Lubābah) bt. al-Ḥārith al-Hilāliyyah. She was the mother of al-Faḍl, ʿAbd Allāh, ʿUbayd Allāh, Maʿbad, Qutham, and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, the sons of al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib ﵁. Her sister was (Maymūnah) bt. al-Ḥārith. She swore allegiance at Mecca before the Hijrah. She had been married to Abū Rahm ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā al-ʿĀmirī.

He died, leaving her a widow. The Messenger of God ﷺ married her. And (Lubābah al-Ṣughrā) bt. al-Ḥārith, the mother of Khālid ibn al-Walīd, swore allegiance before the Hijrah. Their sister, (Huzaylah) bt. al-Ḥārith, swore allegiance after the Hijrah. And (Umm Ḥufayd) al-Hilāliyyah swore allegiance after the Hijrah. (Fāṭimah) bt. Ṣafwān ibn Muḥarrith al-Kināniyyah swore allegiance before the Hijrah. She was the mother of ʿAmr ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ. And (Humaynah) bt. [Khalaf ibn] Asʿad ibn Bayāḍah, a Khuzāʿiyyah, swore allegiance before the Hijrah while with her husband Khālid ibn Saʿīd. She was the mother of Saʿīd ibn Khālid.

And (Amat Allāh) bt. Khālid, the wife of al-Zubayr. She was the mother of ʿAmr and Khālid, the sons of al-Zubayr. (Ḥarmalah) bt. ʿAbd al-Aswad ibn Khuzaymah ibn Abī Qays ibn ʿĀmir ibn Bayāḍah, a Khuzāʿiyyah, was among those who emigrated to Abyssinia. (Fāṭimah) bt. al-Mujallal, also said to be al-Muḥajjal, a Kināniyyah, was married to Ḥāṭib ibn al-Ḥārith al-Jumaḥī. (Ḥasanah), the mother of Sharāḥbīl ibn Ḥasanah, was among the emigrants to Abyssinia. And (Amīnah) bt. Saʿīd ibn Wahb ibn Ashyam, the wife of Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb, swore allegiance at the Conquest.

It is also said that she did so afterward. And (Barzah) bt. Masʿūd al-Thaqafiyyah, the mother of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣafwān the elder, and (al-Baghūm) bt. al-Muʿadhdhal ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣafwān the younger—it is said that they both swore allegiance during the Farewell Pilgrimage. And (Sulāfah) bt. Saʿd al-Shahīd, of Banū ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf, the mother of Ṭalḥah ibn Abī Ṭalḥah’s sons, swore allegiance after the Conquest.

It is also said that she did so afterward. And (Barzah) bt. Masʿūd al-Thaqafiyyah, the mother of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣafwān the elder, and (al-Baghūm) bt. al-Muʿadhdhal ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣafwān the younger—it is said that they both swore allegiance during the Farewell Pilgrimage. And (Sulāfah) bt. Saʿd al-Shahīd, of Banū ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf, the mother of Ṭalḥah ibn Abī Ṭalḥah’s sons, swore allegiance after the Conquest.

And (Umm Ḥakīm) bt. Ṭāriq of Kinānah and (Qutaylah) bt. ʿAmr ibn Hilāl of Kinānah swore allegiance during the Farewell Pilgrimage. And (Umm Maʿbad), ʿĀtikah bt. Khālid ibn Khalīf ibn Munqidh ibn Ḍubays ibn Ḥarām ibn Ḥabashiyyah ibn Kaʿb, of Khuzāʿah. They say she swore allegiance when the Prophet ﷺ passed by her during the Hijrah. It is also said that afterward she came to Medina and swore allegiance after the Hijrah. And (Umm Muṭāʿ) al-Aslamiyyah swore allegiance after the Hijrah. And (Umm Sinān) al-Aslamiyyah swore allegiance after the Hijrah. And (Kuʿaybah) daughter of Saʿd al-Aslamiyya; she gave the pledge after the Hijra. She is the woman who used to tend the sick in the mosque. And (Umm Sunbula) al-Mālikiyya, allied with Aslam. She gave the pledge after the Hijra. And (Umayya) daughter of Abī al-Ṣalt al-Ghifāriyya. She gave the pledge after the Hijra and was present with the Prophet ﷺ—at Khaybar. And (Umm Ṣubayya), Khawla daughter of Qays al-Juhaniyya, gave the pledge after the Hijra. And (Umm Sharīk), Ghaziyya daughter of Jābir, of Azd.

She gave the pledge after the Hijra. And (Fāṭima) daughter of Sawda ibn Abī Ḍubays al-Juhaniyya gave the pledge after the Hijra.

Among the women of the Anṣār, from Banū Ḥāritha:

(Umm ʿUmays) daughter of Maslama ibn Salama ibn Khālid ibn ʿAdī ibn Majdaʿa.

She was the sister of Muḥammad ibn Maslama and the wife of Rāfiʿ ibn Khadīj. She is the woman concerning whom this was revealed: “If a woman fears ill-treatment or desertion from her husband” (4:128). And (Hind) daughter of Maḥmūd ibn Maslama ibn Salama ibn Khālid ibn ʿAdī. And (Umm ʿAmr) daughter of Maḥmūd ibn Maslama ibn Salama ibn Khālid ibn ʿAdī. And (Umm Manẓūr) daughter of Maḥmūd ibn Maslama ibn Salama ibn Khālid ibn ʿAdī. And (Umm al-Rabīʿ) daughter of Aslam ibn Ḥuraysh ibn ʿAdī ibn Majdaʿa, wife of Barḏaʿ ibn Zayd al-Ẓafarī.

She was the mother of Yazīd ibn Barḏaʿ. Abū Saʿīd said: “My recollection is that it is Ḥurays, without a dot, in all these names; Ḥuraysh, with a dot, occurs only among Banū Jaḥjabā, the clan of Uḥayḥa ibn al-Jallāḥ.” And (Suhayma) daughter of Aslam ibn Ḥuraysh ibn ʿAdī ibn Majdaʿa. And (Lubāba) daughter of Aslam ibn Ḥuraysh ibn ʿAdī ibn Majdaʿa.

And (al-Waqṣāʾ) daughter of Masʿūd ibn ʿAbd Saʿd ibn ʿĀmir ibn ʿAdī ibn Jusham ibn Majdaʿa. And (ʿUmayra) daughter of ʿAbd Saʿd ibn ʿĀmir ibn ʿAdī. And (al-Nawwār) daughter of Qays ibn Qays ibn Lawḏān ibn ʿAdī ibn Majdaʿa. And (Umayma) daughter of Abī Ḥaṯma ibn Sāʿida ibn ʿĀmir. She had been married to Laqīṭ ibn ʿAbd Qays, of Banū Fazāra, an ally of Banū Ẓafar. And (Umāma) daughter of Khadīj ibn Rāfiʿ ibn ʿAdī ibn Zayd ibn Jusham, sister of Rāfiʿ ibn Khadīj. And (ʿUmayra) daughter of Ẓuhayr ibn Rāfiʿ ibn ʿAdī ibn Zayd ibn Jusham ibn Ḥāritha. And (Laylā) daughter of Nahīk ibn Isāf ibn ʿAdī ibn Zayd ibn Jusham ibn Ḥāritha.

And (Umm ʿAbd Allāh) daughter of ʿĀzib ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿĀmir ibn Jusham ibn Majdaʿa.

She was al-Barāʾ’s sister. And (Ṯubayta) daughter of al-Rabīʿ ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAdī ibn Zayd ibn Jusham ibn Ḥāritha. And (Laylā) daughter of Rāfiʿ ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAdī ibn Zayd ibn Jusham ibn Ḥāritha, mother of Abū ʿAbs ibn Jabr. And (Jamīla) daughter of Zayd ibn Ṣayfī ibn ʿAmr ibn Zayd ibn Jusham, sister of ʿUlba ibn Zayd. And (Asmāʾ) daughter of Murshid ibn Jabr ibn Mālik ibn Ḥuwayritha ibn Ḥāritha. And (ʿAmra) daughter of Murshid ibn Jabr ibn Mālik ibn Ḥuwayritha, the owner of the Prophet’s lance. And (Jamīla) daughter of Sinān ibn Thaʿlaba ibn ʿĀmir ibn Majdaʿa ibn Jusham ibn Ḥāritha. And (Umayma) daughter of ʿUqba ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAdī ibn Zayd ibn Jusham ibn Ḥāritha.

Among Banū Muʿāwiya:

(Sharafat al-Dār) daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn Qays ibn Haysha. And (Umm Thābit) daughter of Jabr ibn ʿAtīk ibn Qays ibn Haysha.

Among Banū Ẓafar:

(Laylā) daughter of al-Khaṭīm, sister of Qays ibn al-Khaṭīm ibn ʿAdī ibn ʿAmr ibn Sawād. She had been married to Masʿūd ibn Aws ibn Mālik ibn Sawād.

And (Lubnā) daughter of al-Khaṭīm; she had been married to Qays ibn Zayd ibn ʿĀmir ibn Sawād al-Ẓafarī. And (Rayṭa) daughter of al-Khaṭīm. Her account is not firmly established. And (ʿUmayra) daughter of Masʿūd ibn Aws ibn Mālik ibn Sawād, wife of Qays ibn Zayd ibn ʿĀmir ibn Sawād al-Ẓafarī. And (Suhayma) daughter of Masʿūd ibn Aws ibn Mālik ibn Sawād, wife of Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr al-Sulamī. She bore Jābir ʿAbd al-Raḥmān. And (Umm Salama), also said to be Umm Sulaym, daughter of Masʿūd ibn Aws ibn Mālik. She was the mother of al-Muqannaʿ, namely ʿAmr ibn Sinān ibn ʿAmr, of Banū Salāmān ibn Saʿd Huḏaym, an ally of Banū Ẓafar. And (Umm Jundub) daughter of Masʿūd ibn Aws ibn Mālik. She did not marry. And (Ḥabība) daughter of Masʿūd ibn Aws ibn Mālik. She did not marry. And (ʿAmra) daughter of Masʿūd ibn Aws ibn Mālik ibn Sawād. She had been married to Muḥammad ibn Maslama and bore him ʿAbd Allāh. And (Ḥabība) daughter of Qays ibn Zayd ibn ʿĀmir ibn Sawād, mother of al-Ḥārith ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Muʿāḏ ibn ʿAfrāʾ. And (Umm Sahl) daughter of al-Nuʿmān ibn Zayd ibn ʿĀmir ibn Sawād.

And (ʿĀʾisha) daughter of Juẓayy ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿĀmir ibn ʿAbd Razāḥ, wife of Abū al-Munḏir al-Sulamī. She bore him al-Munḏir and ʿAbd al-Raḥmān. Abū al-Munḏir fought at Badr and died during the caliphate of ʿUmar ﵁. His name was Yazīd ibn ʿĀmir ibn Ḥadīda ibn ʿAmr. And (Bashīra) daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd Razāḥ. And (Khalīda) daughter of al-Ḥubāb ibn Juẓayy ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿĀmir ibn ʿAbd Razāḥ. Khalīda had been married to ʿAbd Allāh ibn Saʿd ibn Muʿāḏ but bore him no children. And (Umayma) daughter of al-Nuʿmān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd Razāḥ. And (Shumayla) daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥāritha ibn al-Hayṯam ibn Ẓafar. And (ʿAysāʾ) daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn Sawād ibn al-Hayṯam, mother of Muḥammad ibn Anas ibn Faḍāla. And (Burayda) daughter of Bashīr ibn al-Ḥārith—he was Ubayriq ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥāritha. She had been married to ʿAbbād ibn Nahīk ibn Isāf.

She bore him Ibrāhīm ibn ʿAbbād. And (Ḥabība) daughter of Muʿattib ibn ʿUbayd ibn Sawād ibn al-Hayṯam. She had been married to Bashīr ibn al-Ḥārith and bore him Burayda daughter of Bashīr. And (Umm Sammāk) daughter of Faḍāla ibn ʿAdī ibn Ḥarām ibn al-Hayṯam.

And (Suhayma, ʿAmra, ʿUmayra, Ḥabība, Umm Jundub, and Umm Salama), daughters of Masʿūd ibn Aws ibn Mālik ibn Aws ibn Ẓafar. ʿAmra had been married to Muḥammad ibn Maslama and bore him ʿAbd Allāh. ʿUmayra had been married to Qays ibn Zayd ibn ʿAdī ibn Sawād al-Ẓafarī, paternal uncle of Qatāda ibn al-Nuʿmān. Suhayma had been married to Jābir ibn ʿAbd Allāh and bore him ʿAbd al-Raḥmān. Umm Salama had been married to Sinān ibn ʿAmr, an ally of Banū Ẓafar from Salāmān ibn Saʿd, and bore him al-Muqannaʿ, whose name was ʿAmr. As for Ḥabība and Umm Jundub, they never married. And (Umm ʿAmr), and (ʿUmayra) daughter of al-Khaṭīm, and (Sharafat al-Dār) daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn Qays ibn Haysha al-Muʿāwī—all of these women gave the pledge to the Prophet. And (al-Shamūs) daughter of ʿAmr ibn Ḥarām, sister of ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥarām.

Among Banū ʿAbd al-Ashhal:

(al-Rabāb) daughter of al-Nuʿmān ibn Imruʾ al-Qays ibn Zayd ibn ʿAbd al-Ashhal. She was the mother of Muʿāḏ ibn Zurāra al-Ẓafarī and al-Barāʾ ibn Maʿrūr al-Sulamī.

And (ʿAqrab) daughter of Muʿāḏ ibn al-Nuʿmān ibn Imruʾ al-Qays ibn Zayd ibn ʿAbd al-Ashhal, mother of Rāfiʿ ibn Yazīd al-Ashhalī, who fought at Badr, and of Yazīd and Thābit, sons of Qays ibn al-Khaṭīm. And (Hind) daughter of Sammāk ibn ʿAtīk ibn Imruʾ al-Qays ibn Zayd.

She was the mother of ʿAmr and ʿAbd Allāh, sons of Saʿd ibn Muʿāḏ. She was also the paternal aunt of Usayd ibn Ḥuḍayr. And (Umāma) daughter of Sammāk ibn ʿAtīk. She was the mother of al-Ḥārith ibn Aws ibn Muʿāḏ. And (Fukayha), also called Umm ʿĀmir, daughter of Yazīd ibn al-Sakan ibn Rāfiʿ ibn Imruʾ al-Qays. And (Umm Sharīk) daughter of Anas ibn Rāfiʿ ibn Imruʾ al-Qays ibn Zayd. And (Ḥawwāʾ) daughter of Rāfiʿ ibn Imruʾ al-Qays ibn Zayd. And (Umm Saʿd) daughter of ʿUqba ibn Rāfiʿ ibn Imruʾ al-Qays; she was the paternal aunt of Maḥmūd ibn Labīd ibn ʿUqba. And (Umm al-Ḥakam) daughter of ʿUqba ibn Rāfiʿ ibn Imruʾ al-Qays ibn Zayd. And (ʿAmra) daughter of Yazīd ibn al-Sakan ibn Rāfiʿ ibn Imruʾ al-Qays.

And (ʿAqrab) daughter of Salāma ibn Waqsh ibn Zugba ibn Zaʿūrāʾ. And (Umm Ḥanẓala) daughter of Rūmī ibn Waqsh ibn Zugba ibn Zaʿūrāʾ. And (Ḥawwāʾ) daughter of Yazīd ibn al-Sakan ibn Kurayz ibn Zaʿūrāʾ. She was the wife of Qays ibn al-Khaṭīm, for whose care the Prophet commended her. And (ʿAbbāda) daughter of Abī Nāʾila Sulkān ibn Salāma ibn Waqsh ibn Zugba ibn Zaʿūrāʾ. And (Umm Sahl) daughter of Rūmī ibn Waqsh ibn Zugba ibn Zaʿūrāʾ. And (Umāma) daughter of Bishr ibn Waqsh ibn Zaʿba, sister of ʿAbbād. She was the mother of ʿAlī ibn Asad ibn ʿUbayd ibn Saʿiya ibn al-Hadl. Their place of residence was among Qurayẓa. And (Umm ʿAmr) daughter of Salāma ibn Waqsh ibn Zugba, wife of Muḥammad ibn Maslama. And (al-Rabāb) daughter of Kaʿb ibn ʿAdī ibn Kaʿb ibn ʿAbd al-Ashhal. She was the mother of Ḥuḏayfa ibn al-Yamān, Saʿd ibn al-Yamān, and Ṣafwān ibn al-Yamān. And (Umm Niyār) daughter of Zayd ibn Mālik ibn ʿAdī ibn Kaʿb, sister of Saʿd ibn Zayd. And (Umayma) daughter of ʿAmr ibn Sahl ibn Saʿīd ibn Qalʿ ibn Ḥuraysh ibn ʿAbd al-Ashhal. And (Umayma) daughter of Mālik ibn al-Tayyihān. And (Hind) daughter of Sahl ibn ʿAmr ibn Zayd ibn ʿAmr ibn Jusham, from the people of Rātiʿ. And (Mulayka) daughter of ʿAmr ibn Sahl, also from among them; she was the wife of Abū al-Hayṯam ibn al-Tayyihān. And (al-Ṣaʿbaʾ) daughter of Sahl ibn ʿAmr ibn Zayd ibn ʿAmr.

Among Banū ʿAmr ibn ʿAwf:

(al-Shamūs) daughter of Abī ʿĀmir, whose name was ʿAbd ʿAmr ibn Ṣayfī ibn Zayd ibn Amat ibn Ḍubayʿa. She was the mother of ʿĀṣim and Jamīla, the children of Thābit ibn Abī al-Aqlaḥ. And (Jamīla) daughter of Abī ʿĀmir, namely ʿAbd ʿAmr ibn Ṣayfī ibn Zayd ibn Amat ibn Ḍubayʿa. And (Laylā) daughter of Abī Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Qays ibn Zayd ibn Amat. And (Tamīma) daughter of Abī Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Qays ibn Zayd ibn Amat. And (ʿĀʾisha) daughter of Abī Sufyān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Qays ibn Zayd ibn Amat. And (Umm Jamīl) daughter of al-Jallās ibn Suwayd ibn al-Ṣāmit.

And (Jamīla) daughter of [Thābit] ibn Abī al-Aqlaḥ ibn ʿIṣma. She had been married to ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb and bore him ʿĀṣim. She is the woman who disputed over her son before Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq ﵁. ʿUmar ﵁ then divorced her, and Yazīd ibn Ḥāritha married her; she bore him ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Yazīd. And (Nusayba) daughter of Sammāk ibn al-Nuʿmān ibn Qays ibn Zayd ibn Amat. And (Ḥafṣa) daughter of Ḥāṭib ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUbayd ibn Umayya ibn Zayd, sister of al-Ḥārith ibn Ḥāṭib. And (Saʿīda) daughter of Rifāʿa ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUbayd ibn Umayya. And (Muṭīʿa) daughter of al-Nuʿmān ibn Mālik.

Her name had been ʿĀṣiya. And (Ṯubayta) daughter of Yaʿār, from Banū ʿUbayd, wife of Abū Ḥuḏayfa.

She is the woman who emancipated Sālim. And (Sulaymā) daughter of Yaʿār. And (ʿUmayra) daughter of ʿUbayd ibn Maṭrūf ibn al-Ḥārith, from Zayd ibn ʿUbayd. And (Unaysa) daughter of Sāʿida ibn ʿĀʾish ibn Qays ibn al-Nuʿmān, sister of ʿUwaym ibn Sāʿida.

And (ʿUmayra) daughter of ʿUwaym ibn Sāʿida ibn ʿĀʾish ibn Qays ibn al-Nuʿmān.

And (ʿUmayra) daughter of Kulṯūm ibn al-Hadm ibn Imruʾ al-Qays ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Zayd ibn ʿUbayd.

Among Banū Jaḥjabā:

(Nusayba) daughter of Niyār ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Bilāl ibn Uḥayḥa.

And (ʿUmayra) daughter of ʿUqba ibn Uḥayḥa. And (Ṯubayta) daughter of Maʿbad ibn Sahl ibn Uḥayḥa. And (al-Furayʿa) daughter of Qays ibn ʿUmayr ibn Lawḏān ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Majdaʿa ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥuraysh ibn Jaḥjabā.

Among Banū al-Silm:

(Khayra) daughter of Abī Umayya ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Mālik ibn Kaʿb ibn al-Naḥḥāṭ.

Among Banū Khaṭma:

(Unaysa) daughter of Abī Ṭalḥa ibn ʿIṣma ibn Zayd. And (Rābiʿa) daughter of Thābit ibn al-Fākih ibn Thaʿlaba. And (Rifāʿa) daughter of Thābit ibn al-Fākih ibn Thaʿlaba. And (Mulayka) daughter of al-Fākih ibn Thaʿlaba. And (ʿUmāra) daughter of Ḥubāsha. And (Kabsha) daughter of Aws ibn Shurayq. She was the mother of Khuzayma ibn Thābit. And (Unaysa) daughter of Raqīm. And (Hind) daughter of Aws ibn Shurayq, mother of Saʿd ibn Khayṯama. And (Laylā) daughter of Aws ibn Shurayq. And (Umm al-Fuhayḏ) daughter of Ḥubāsha. And (Ṣafiyya) daughter of Thābit ibn [ʿUmāra ibn] al-Fākih ibn Thaʿlaba.

Among the Jaʿāḏira:

The Jaʿāḏira are the descendants of Murra ibn Mālik ibn al-Aws. They are Banū Umayya ibn Zayd, ʿAṭiyya ibn Zayd, and Wāʾil ibn Zayd, the clan of Abū Qays ibn al-Aslat.

And the descendants of Saʿīd ibn Murra who are among Banū ʿAbd al-Ashhal.

(Salmā) daughter of Zayd ibn Taym ibn Umayya ibn [Khafāf ibn] Bayāḍa ibn Khufāf, from Saʿīd ibn Murra ibn Mālik ibn al-Aws; they are among Banū ʿAbd al-Ashhal.

Among Balḥārith ibn Kaʿb ibn al-Khazraj:

(Kabsha) daughter of Wāqid ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Iṭnāba ibn ʿAmr. She was the mother of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Rawāḥa. Her daughter was ʿAmra bt. Rawāḥa ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Imruʾ al-Qays, the mother of al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr. Also, Hudhayla bt. ʿAmr ibn ʿUtba ibn Khudayj ibn ʿĀmir ibn Jusham ibn al-Ḥārith ibn al-Khazraj; she was the mother of Saʿd ibn al-Rabīʿ. And Muḥibba bt. al-Rabīʿ ibn ʿAmr ibn Abī Zuhayr, Saʿd’s sister. And Laylā bt. Simāk ibn Thābit ibn Sufyān ibn Jusham ibn ʿAmr ibn Imruʾ al-Qays.

And Ḥabība bt. Khārija ibn Zayd ibn Abī Zuhayr, the wife of Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq ﵀. And Hudhayla bt. Thābit ibn Thaʿlaba ibn al-Jallās. And Jamīla bt. Saʿd ibn al-Rabīʿ ibn ʿAmr ibn Abī Zuhayr. And Zaynab bt. Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās. And Umm Thābit bt. Qays ibn Shammās. And Umm Ayyūb bt. Qays ibn Saʿd ibn Qays ibn ʿAmr ibn Imruʾ al-Qays. And Mandūs bt. Khallād ibn Suwayd ibn Thaʿlaba. And Umayma bt. Bashīr ibn Saʿd ibn Thaʿlaba, the sister of al-Nuʿmān ibn Bashīr. And Unaysa bt. Thaʿlaba ibn Zayd ibn Qays.

Among the Banū Jusham

Umm Zayd bt. al-Sakan ibn ʿUtba ibn ʿAmr ibn Khudayj. And Unaysa bt. Khubayb ibn Isāf. And Qarība bt. Zayd ibn ʿAbd Rabbih ibn Zayd.

Among the Banū Jadāra

Muʿādha bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Muzayn ibn Qays. And Kabsha bt. Thābit ibn Ḥāritha ibn Thaʿlaba ibn al-Jallās. And Umm al-Ḥakam bt. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Masʿūd ibn Thaʿlaba.

Among the Banū al-Abjar

Saʿd ibn Muʿādh’s mother: Kabsha bt. Rāfiʿ ibn Muʿāwiya ibn ʿUbayd ibn al-Abjar. And al-Furayʿa bt. Rāfiʿ ibn Muʿāwiya ibn ʿUbayd ibn al-Abjar, the mother of Saʿd ibn Zurāra. And al-Furayʿa bt. al-Ḥubāb. And ʿAfrāʾ bt. al-Sakan ibn Rāfiʿ ibn Muʿāwiya ibn ʿUbayd ibn al-Abjar. And al-Rubāb bt. Ḥāritha ibn Sinān ibn ʿUbayd. And Khulayda bt. Thābit ibn Sinān ibn ʿUbayd.

And al-Rabīʿ bt. Ḥāritha ibn Sinān ibn ʿUbayd. And Umm Thābit bt. Thābit ibn Sinān ibn ʿUbayd.

Among the Banū Sāʿida

Mandūs bt. ʿAmr ibn Khunays ibn Lawdhān ibn ʿAbd Wudd, the sister of al-Mundhir ibn ʿAmr. She was the mother of Salama ibn Mukhlad. And Umm Unās bt. Khālid ibn Khunays ibn Lawdhān ibn ʿAbd Wudd, the mother of Muḥammad al-Najjārī. And al-Furayʿa bt. ʿAmr ibn Khunays ibn Lawdhān. She was the mother of Ḥassān ibn Thābit. And Umm Sharīk bt. Khālid ibn Khunays ibn Lawdhān ibn ʿAbd Wudd. She was the mother of al-Ḥārith ibn Anas al-Ashhalī. And Salmā bt. ʿAmr ibn Khunays ibn Lawdhān ibn ʿAbd Wudd, the sister of al-Mundhir. And ʿAbda bt. Saʿd ibn Mālik, the paternal aunt of Sahl ibn Saʿd.

And ʿAmra bt. Saʿd ibn Saʿd ibn Mālik, Sahl ibn Saʿd’s sister. She was the mother of Rifāʿa ibn Mubashshir ibn Ubayriq al-Ẓafarī. And Nāʾila bt. Saʿd ibn Saʿd ibn Mālik.

Among the Banū Ṭarīf ibn al-Khazraj ibn Sāʿida

Fukayha bt. ʿAbd ibn Dulaym ibn Ḥāritha, the mother of Qays ibn Saʿd.

And Mandūs bt. ʿUbāda ibn Dulaym ibn Ḥāritha ibn Abī Khuzayma, the sister of Saʿd ibn ʿUbāda. And ʿAdiyya bt. Saʿd ibn Khalīfa ibn al-Ashraf. She was the mother of Saʿīd ibn Saʿd. And Laylā bt. ʿUbāda ibn Dulaym ibn Ḥāritha. And Kubaysha bt. ʿAbd ʿAmr ibn ʿUbayd ibn Qumayʾa ibn ʿĀmir ibn al-Khazraj ibn Sāʿida.

They are also called the Banū Qishba.

Among the Banū ʿAwf ibn al-Khazraj ibn Ḥāritha, the Qawāqil

Ḥabība bt. Mulayl ibn Wabara ibn Khālid ibn al-ʿAjlān. And Qurrat al-ʿAyn bt. ʿUbāda ibn Naḍla ibn al-ʿAjlān, the mother of ʿUbāda ibn al-Ṣāmit.

And Bashīra bt. Mulayl ibn Wabara ibn Khālid ibn al-ʿAjlān. And Furayʿa bt. Abī Ḥāritha ibn Aws ibn al-Dukhshum. And Umm Anas bt. Wāqid ibn ʿAmr ibn Marḍaḥa. And al-Furayʿa bt. Mālik ibn al-Dukhshum ibn Mālik.

And Jamīla bt. Khuzayma. And Laylā bt. Riʾāb ibn Ḥunayf. And ʿAmra bt. Hazzāl ibn ʿAmr ibn Qurawāsh. And Khawla bt. Ṣāmit ibn Aṣram ibn Fihr. And Umāma bt. Ṣāmit ibn Aṣram ibn Fihr. And Khawla bt. Thaʿlaba ibn Aṣram ibn Fihr; she is “the woman who disputed,” the wife of Aws ibn al-Ṣāmit.

Among the Banū al-Ḥublī, who are called the Laḥublī

Jamīla bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl. She was the mother of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥanẓala and the mother of Muḥammad ibn Thābit ibn Qays ibn Shammās. He was killed with his brother on the Day of al-Ḥarra. And Malikiyya bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl, the wife of Hilāl ibn Umayya al-Wāqifī. And Ramla bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl. And Umm Saʿd bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl. And Khawla bt. Khawliyy ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith; she was the sister of Aws ibn Khawliyy. And Fasḥam bt. Aws ibn Khawliyy ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith. And Zaynab bt. Sahl ibn al-Ṣaʿb ibn Qays.

And Umm Saʿd bt. Ubayy ibn Mālik ibn ʿUbayd ibn Sālim. She was the mother of Rifāʿa ibn Rāfiʿ al-Zuraqī. And Laylā bt. al-Iṭnāba ibn Muʿayṣ ibn Jusham.

Among the Banū Zurayq

Umm Qays bt. Ḥiṣn, the sister of Qays ibn Ḥiṣn. And Umm Saʿd bt. Qays ibn Ḥiṣn. And Ḥabba bt. ʿAmr ibn Ḥiṣn. And Unaysa bt. Muʿādh ibn Māʿiṣ ibn Qays ibn Khilda. And Sunbula bt. Māʿiṣ ibn Qays ibn Khilda. And Umm Saʿd bt. Masʿūd ibn Saʿd ibn Qays ibn Khilda. And Umm Thābit bt. Masʿūd ibn Saʿd ibn Qays. And Umm Sahl bt. Masʿūd ibn Saʿd ibn Qays ibn Khilda. And Umāma bt. ʿUthmān ibn Khilda ibn Mukhlad, the sister of Abū ʿUbāda. And Umm Rāfiʿ bt. ʿUthmān ibn Khilda ibn Mukhlad. And Fukayha bt. al-Muṭṭalib ibn Khilda ibn Mukhlad. And Nufaysa bt. ʿAmr ibn Khilda ibn Mukhlad. And Ḥabība bt. Masʿūd ibn Khilda.

Among the Banū Bayāḍa

Laylā bt. Rubayʿ ibn ʿĀmir ibn Khilda. And Khawla bt. Mālik ibn Anas ibn Thaʿlaba. And Unaysa bt. ʿUrwa ibn Masʿūd ibn Sinān ibn ʿĀmir ibn Umayya ibn Bayāḍa. And Thubayta bt. al-Nuʿmān ibn ʿAmr ibn al-Nuʿmān ibn Khilda ibn ʿAmr. And Jamīla bt. ʿUrwa ibn Masʿūd ibn Sinān.

And Kabsha bt. Farwa ibn ʿAmr ibn Wadhfa. And Umāma bt. Qarība ibn al-ʿAjlān ibn Ghanm ibn ʿĀmir ibn Bayāḍa. And al-Fāriʿa bt. Qarība ibn al-ʿAjlān ibn Ghanm ibn ʿĀmir ibn Bayāḍa. And Umm Sharḥabīl bt. Farwa ibn ʿAmr. And Khilda bt. ʿAmr ibn Wadhfa. And Āmina bt. Khalīfa ibn ʿAdī ibn ʿAmr ibn Mālik. And Unaysa bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Mālik ibn al-ʿAjlān. And Unaysa bt. Hilāl ibn al-Muʿallā ibn Lawdhān.

And Unaysa bt. Rāfiʿ ibn al-Muʿallā ibn Lawdhān.

Among the Banū Ḥarām

al-Shamūs bt. ʿAmr ibn Ḥarām ibn Zayd, the mother of the daughters of Masʿūd ibn Aws al-Ẓafariyyāt. And Idām bt. al-Jamūḥ ibn Zayd ibn Ḥarām ibn Zayd, the mother of Ḥuwayṣa, Muḥayyiṣa, and al-Aḥwaṣ al-Ḥārithiyyūn. And Hind bt. al-Mundhir ibn al-Jamūḥ ibn Zayd, the mother of al-Mundhir ibn ʿAmr al-Sāʿidī. And Lumays bt. ʿUmar ibn Ḥarām ibn Zayd. And Umm Muʿādh bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥarām. And Hind bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥarām. And Umm Jamīl bt. al-Ḥubāb ibn al-Mundhir ibn Jamūḥ. And Umm al-Ḥārith bt. Thābit al-Jadhaʿ.

And ʿĀʾisha bt. ʿUmayr ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Thaʿlaba. And Umayma bt. ʿAmr ibn al-Jamūḥ ibn Zayd. And Umm Thaʿlaba bt. Thābit al-Jadhaʿ. And Ḥumayma bt. Ḥammām ibn al-Jamūḥ ibn Zayd. And al-Rubāb and Salāma, daughters of al-Barāʾ ibn Maʿrūr ibn Khansāʾ. And Ḥumayma bt. Ṣayfī ibn Ṣakhr ibn Khansāʾ. And Zaynab bt. Ṣayfī ibn Ṣakhr ibn Khansāʾ. And Qubaysa bt. Ṣayfī ibn Ṣakhr ibn Khansāʾ.

And ʿUmayra bt. Qurṭ ibn Khansāʾ ibn Sinān. And Asmāʾ bt. Qurṭ ibn Khansāʾ ibn Sinān. And Idām bt. Qurṭ ibn Khansāʾ ibn Sinān. And Umāma bt. Qurṭ ibn Khansāʾ ibn Sinān. And Āmina bt. Qurṭ ibn Khansāʾ ibn Sinān. And Umāma bt. Muḥarrith ibn Zayd ibn Thaʿlaba. And Umm al-Ḥārith bt. Mālik ibn Khansāʾ ibn Sinān. And Khansāʾ bt. Ziyād ibn al-Nuʿmān ibn Sinān. And Umm Zayd bt. Qays ibn al-Nuʿmān ibn Sinān. And ʿUṣayma bt. Jabbār ibn Ṣakhr ibn Khansāʾ. And Hudhayla bt. Masʿūd ibn Zayd. And Suʿād bt. Salama ibn Zuhayr ibn Thaʿlaba. She was the one who asked the Messenger of God ﷺ—to accept her pledge concerning the child in her womb, for she was pregnant. He ﷺ—said to her: “You are free among the freeborn women.”

Among the Banū Sawād

ʿAmra bt. Qays ibn Abī Kaʿb ibn al-Qayn, the sister of Sahl ibn Qays, who was martyred at Uḥud. And Umm Jamīl bt. Quṭba ibn ʿĀmir ibn Ḥadīda.

Among the Banū Māzin

Nusayba bt. Kaʿb ibn ʿAmr, Umm ʿUmāra. And Jamīla bt. Abī Ṣaʿṣaʿa. And Athīla bt. al-Ḥārith ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Ṣakhr ibn Ḥarām.

Among the Banū ʿAdī

Umm Sulaym bt. Milḥān ibn Zayd ibn Ḥarām. And Umm Ḥarām bt.

And Khawla bt. al-Mundhir ibn Zayd ibn Lubayd ibn Khudāsh. She was the one who nursed Ibrāhīm, the son of the Messenger of God ﷺ. She was the mother of Burda. And Unaysa bt. Qays Abī Khārija ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿa ibn Wahb, the mother of Qatāda ibn al-Nuʿmān and Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī. And Umm ʿUbayd bt. Surāqa ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAdī.

And Umm Sahl bt. Abī Khārija, who was ʿAmr ibn Qays. And Thubayta bt. Sulayṭ ibn Qays. And Umm Khārija bt. al-Naḍr ibn Ḍamḍam. And Umm Ḥakīm bt. al-Naḍr ibn Ḍamḍam. And al-Nawwār bt. Mālik ibn Ṣarma ibn Abī Anas, the mother of Zayd ibn Thābit. And Umm al-Mundhir bt. Qays ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUbayd, and Umm Sulaym bt. Qays ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUbayd, and ʿUmayra bt. Qays ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUbayd ibn Mālik ibn ʿAdī, the sisters of Sulayṭ ibn Qays. And Asmāʾ bt. Muḥriz ibn ʿĀmir ibn ʿAdī. And Salmā bt. Muḥriz ibn ʿĀmir. And Ḥawwāʾ bt. Thābit ibn Zayd ibn Qays ibn Sakan.

Among the Banū Dīnār

Mandūs bt. Quṭba ibn ʿAbd ʿAmr ibn Masʿūd. And Hudhayla bt. Saʿīd ibn Sahl ibn Mālik ibn Kaʿb. And al-Sumaydaʿ bt. Qays ibn Mālik ibn Kaʿb.

Among the Banū Mālik

Jaʿda bt. ʿUbayd ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Sawād ibn Ghanm, the mother of Ḥāritha ibn al-Nuʿmān. And ʿAfrāʾ bt. ʿUbayd ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Sawād ibn Ghanm ibn ʿUbayd, the mother of Muʿādh and Muʿawwidh. And Ḥarmala bt. ʿUbayd ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Sawād ibn Ghanm.

And Khawla bt. Qays ibn Fahd ibn Qays ibn Thaʿlaba ibn ʿUbayd, the wife of Ḥamza ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib ﵀. And al-Rabīʿ bt. Muʿawwidh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Rifāʿa.

And ʿAmra bt. Muʿawwidh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Rifāʿa. And ʿAmra bt. Masʿūd ibn Zurāra ibn ʿAdas. And ʿAtaba bt. Zurāra ibn ʿAdas. And al-Fāriʿa bt. Zurāra ibn ʿAdas. And Ramla bt. al-Ḥārith ibn Thaʿlaba ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Zayd. He is “al-Ḥadath.” And Laylā bt. Thābit ibn al-Mundhir ibn Ḥarām. And Kabsha bt. Thābit ibn al-Mundhir ibn Ḥarām, the sister of Ḥassān. And Umm al-Rabīʿ bt. ʿUbayd ibn al-Nuʿmān ibn ʿUtayk.

And Umm Zayd bt. ʿAmr ibn Ḥarām ibn ʿAmr, the owner of the camel that came to the Prophet ﷺ. And ʿAmra bt. Masʿūd ibn Qays ibn ʿAmr ibn Zayd Manāt. And Umm Simāk bt. Thābit. And Umm Salama bt. Rāfiʿ ibn Abī ʿAmr. And Suʿād bt. Rāfiʿ ibn Abī ʿAmr ibn Thaʿlaba. And (Umm Khālid), daughter of Yaʿīsh ibn Qays ibn ʿAmr. And (ʿAmra), daughter of al-Rabīʿ ibn al-Nuʿmān ibn Yasāf. And (Umm Sulaym), daughter of Khālid ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Suḥaym. And (Ruqayya), daughter of Thābit ibn Khālid ibn al-Nuʿmān. And (ʿAmra), daughter of Ḥazm ibn Zayd ibn Lawdhān, sister of ʿAmr, ʿUmāra, and Maʿmar, the sons of Ḥazm.

And (ʿAmra), daughter of Abū Ayyūb Khālid ibn Zayd ibn Kulayb. And (Umm Thābit), daughter of Thaʿlaba ibn Miḥṣan. And (Ḍubāʿa), daughter of ʿAmr ibn Miḥṣan. And (Umm Sahl), daughter of Sahl ibn ʿAtīk ibn al-Nuʿmān. And (Umm Saʿd), daughter of Thābit ibn ʿAtīk ibn al-Nuʿmān. And (Umm Jamīl), daughter of Abū Ḥazm ibn ʿAtīk ibn al-Nuʿmān. And (Ḥabība), daughter of Asʿad ibn Zurāra. She was the mother of Abū Umāma ibn Sahl ibn Ḥunayf.

And (al-Furayʿa), daughter of Asʿad ibn Zurāra ibn ʿAdas. And (Kabsha), daughter of Asʿad ibn Zurāra ibn ʿAdas. And (ʿAmra), daughter of Masʿūd ibn Qays ibn ʿAmr ibn Zayd Manāt. And (ʿAmra), daughter of Masʿūd ibn Qays ibn ʿAmr, who was the mother of Saʿīd ibn Saʿd ibn ʿUbāda ibn Dulaym. And (ʿAmra), daughter of Masʿūd ibn Qays ibn ʿAmr. She had no children. And (ʿAmra), daughter of Masʿūd ibn Qays ibn ʿAmr, who was the mother of Abū Shaykh ibn Thābit, Ḥassān’s brother.

[And (ʿAmra), daughter of Masʿūd ibn Qays ibn ʿAmr, who was the mother of Qays ibn ʿAmr al-Najjārī.] They were five daughters of Masʿūd ibn Qays ibn ʿAmr ibn Zayd Manāt, all of whom were called “ʿAmra.” / One was the mother of Saʿīd ibn Saʿd ibn ʿUbāda.

Another was the mother of Saʿīd ibn Zayd al-Ashhalī. Another was the mother of Ubayy ibn Thābit, Abū Shaykh.

Another was the mother of Qays ibn ʿAmr al-Najjārī. Another had no children.

She was of Banū Judayla.

Women who joined the polytheists, whose husbands had their bridal payments, which they had received from them, reimbursed by the Messenger of God ﷺ

Concerning them was revealed: “And if any of your wives have gone from you to the unbelievers, and you then gain an advantage, give those whose wives have gone away the equivalent of what they had spent” (Qurʾān 60:11). They were six women. (Umm al-Ḥakam), daughter of Abū Sufyān, had been married to ʿIyāḍ ibn Ghanm ibn Shaddād al-Fihrī. And (Qurayba), namely Fāṭima, daughter of Abū Umayya ibn al-Mughīra al-Makhzūmī, had been married to ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb ﵁. When he intended to emigrate, he urged her to emigrate with him, but she refused. He left her and emigrated. Afterward, Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān married her. The Messenger of God ﷺ—then gave ʿUmar her bridal payment and what he had spent on her. A daughter of ʿUtba ibn Rabīʿa, named (Barūʿ), had been married to Shimās ibn ʿUthmān al-Makhzūmī. And (Hind)—also said to be ʿAmra—daughter of ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn Naḍla, was married to ʿAmr ibn ʿAbd ʿAmr Dhū al-Shimālayn, of Khuzāʿa. And (Hind), daughter of Abū Jahl, had been married to Hishām ibn al-ʿĀṣ ibn Wāʾil al-Sahmī. And (Umm Kulthūm), daughter of Jarwal ibn Mālik ibn al-Musayyib al-Khuzāʿī, had also been married to ʿUmar. The Messenger of God ﷺ—gave these women’s husbands their wives’ bridal payments from the spoils.

Women who granted protection

(Khumāʿa), daughter of ʿAwf ibn Muḥallim al-Shaybānī, granted protection to Marwān ibn Zinbāʿ al-ʿAbsī. And (Fukayha), daughter of Qatāda ibn Mashnūʾ, of Banū Mālik ibn Ḍubayʿa, one of the Banū Qays ibn Thaʿlaba, granted protection to al-Sulayk ibn al-Salaka al-Saʿdī.

He was a man [of notable courage]. He was the one called “the lion.” He raided Bakr ibn Wāʾil but found no opening to exploit. They saw a footprint they did not recognize and said, “This is the footprint of a man who comes to the watering place, someone we do not know.” So they lay in wait for him, allowing time for him to drink; once he had drunk his fill, they would attack him. They did so. Al-Sulayk arrived when the sun stood at midday. He took off his face covering, plunged his mouth into the trough, and drank until he was full. He began pouring water over his face and head. They rushed at him, and his belly grew heavy. He ran until he entered Fukayha’s tent, and sought her protection. She hid him beneath her mantle. They came after him, pursuing him, and she defended him until they pulled off her veil. She called out to her brothers and sons, and they came and drove the men away from her.

Al-Sulayk praised her, saying:

By your father’s life! The reports spread—

What an excellent neighbor, sister of Banū ʿAwāra!

One of the modest women, she did not disgrace her father,

Nor bring shame upon her brothers.

Fukayha was not powerless on the day she stood,

With the sword’s edge, while they snatched away the veil.

And (Umm Jamīl) al-Dawsiyya granted protection to Ḍirār ibn al-Khaṭṭāb al-Fihrī. Her loyalty was shown when Hishām ibn al-Walīd ibn al-Mughīra al-Makhzūmī had killed Abū Uẓayhir al-Dawsī. He was related by marriage to Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb ibn Umayya, and there is an account about him.

When his people heard of this, they sprang upon Ḍirār ibn al-Khaṭṭāb al-Fihrī to kill him.

He fled until he entered Umm Jamīl’s house and sought her protection. One of them struck him, and the flat of the sword landed on the door. She stood before them and defended him, calling out to her people. They protected him. When ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb ﵁—became caliph, she thought he was his brother and came to him in Medina. When she gave her lineage, he recognized the story and said to her, “I am his brother only in Islam; he is on campaign. We know what you did for him.” He gave her something as though she were a wayfarer.

Names of women who married three or more husbands

First is Māriya, daughter of al-Juʿayd ibn Ṣabra ibn al-Dayl ibn Shann ibn Afṣā ibn ʿAbd al-Qays. She married (Qays) ibn Thaʿlaba ibn ʿUkāba. Then (Ḥanīfa) ibn Lujaym ibn ʿĀmir ibn Ḥanīfa. Then (Saʿd) ibn ʿAjl ibn Lujaym ibn Thaʿlaba.

Then (Thaʿlaba) ibn Ghanm ibn Ḥabīb ibn Kaʿb ibn Yashkur ibn Bakr. [Then] (Mālik) ibn Ḍamra ibn Bakr ibn ʿAbd Manāt ibn Kināna. This Māriya married (Imruʾ al-Qays) ibn Buhtha ibn Sulaym, and bore him Khufāf, ʿAwf, and Bahz.

She was also married to (Thaʿlaba) ibn Mālik ibn Marbūs ibn Ṭarīf ibn al-Namir ibn Yaqdam ibn ʿAnaza. She married (Ghālib) ibn ʿAdī ibn Shumays ibn Ṭarūd ibn Qudāma ibn Jarm. She married (Imruʾ al-Qays) ibn Zayd Manāt ibn Tamīm.

She married (ʿUdhrā) ibn Saʿd Hudhaym.

Umm Khārija, namely ʿAmra, daughter of Saʿd ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Qudād al-Bajaliyya, married (a man from Iyād). Then (Bakr) ibn Yashkur ibn ʿAdwān married her.

Then (ʿAmr) ibn Rabīʿa ibn Ḥāritha ibn ʿAmr Muzayqiyā. Then (Bakr) ibn ʿAbd Manāt ibn Kināna. [Then] (Mālik) ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Dūdān ibn Asad. [Then] (Jusham) ibn Mālik ibn Kaʿb ibn al-Qayn ibn Jasr. Then (ʿĀmir) ibn ʿAmr ibn Laḥyūn al-Bahrānī. Then (ʿAmr) ibn Tamīm ibn Murr.

/ And Dukhtanūs, daughter of Laqīṭ ibn Zurāra, married (ʿAmr) ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿAdas. Then (ʿUmayr) ibn Maʿbad ibn Zurāra. Then (Muslim) ibn ʿUbayd ibn Yarbuʿ ibn [Thaʿlaba ibn] al-Dūl ibn Ḥanīfa.

Māwiya al-Mutajarrida al-Kalbiyya, daughter of al-Mundhir ibn al-Aswad, married (Jalm), namely Aswad ibn al-Mundhir ibn Ḥāritha al-Kalbī, who then divorced her. She married (al-Mundhir) ibn al-Mundhir ibn Ḥāritha al-Kalbī, who then divorced her. She married (al-Mundhir) ibn al-Mundhir. Then his son (al-Nuʿmān) ibn al-Mundhir al-Lakhmī married her.

Hind, daughter of ʿUtba ibn Rabīʿa, married (al-Fākih) ibn al-Mughīra ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn Makhzūm. He was killed at al-Ghumayṣāʾ. Then she married (Ḥafṣ) ibn al-Mughīra. He died. Then she married (Abū Sufyān) Ṣakhr ibn Ḥarb ibn Umayya.

Umm Kulthūm, daughter of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib ﵁—married (ʿUmar) ibn al-Khaṭṭāb ﵀. Then (ʿAwn) ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib married her. Then (Muḥammad) ibn Jaʿfar. [Then] (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Jaʿfar.

ʿĀtika, daughter of Zayd ibn ʿAmr ibn Nufayl ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā, married (ʿUbayda) ibn al-Ḥārith ibn al-Muṭṭalib. Then (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Abī Bakr.

Then (ʿUmar) ibn al-Khaṭṭāb. Then (al-Zubayr) ibn al-ʿAwwām. Then (Muḥammad) ibn Abī Bakr, who was killed while she was married to him in Egypt. Then (ʿAmr) ibn al-ʿĀṣ al-Sahmī.

Umm al-Ḥasan, daughter of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, married (Jaʿda) ibn Hubayra ibn Abī Wahb al-Makhzūmī. Then (Jaʿfar) ibn ʿAqīl ibn Abī Ṭālib married her. He was killed with al-Ḥusayn. Then (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām.

Umm al-Qāsim, daughter of al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, married (Marwān) ibn Abān ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān. Then (ʿAlī) ibn al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī married her. Then (al-Ḥusayn) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

Sukayna, daughter of al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, married (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī, who was her first husband. He died while she was married to him. Then (Muṣʿab) ibn al-Zubayr married her, and she bore him Fāṭima. She died when she was young.

He was killed while she was married to him. / (ʿAbd al-Malik) ibn Marwān proposed to her, but she refused him. She married (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥakīm ibn Ḥizām ibn Khuwaylid. Then (al-Aṣbagh) ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Marwān married her, but he did not consummate the marriage: he divorced her before that.

Then (Zayd) ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān. Then (Ibrāhīm) ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf, but he did not consummate the marriage, and she was not satisfied with him. She was given the choice, and chose herself.

Umm ʿAlī, daughter of ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī, married (ʿAlī) ibn al-Ḥusayn ibn al-Ḥasan. Then (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Muʿāwiya ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar married her. Then (Muḥammad) ibn Muʿāwiya ibn ʿAbd Allāh. Then (Nūḥ) ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh.

Ḍubāʿa, daughter of ʿĀmir ibn Qurṭ al-Qushayriyya, married (Hawdha) ibn ʿAlī al-Ḥanafī. Then (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn Judʿān al-Taymī. Then (Hishām) ibn al-Mughīra al-Makhzūmī.

Umm Kulthūm, daughter of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib, married (al-Qāsim) ibn Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar. Then (al-Ḥajjāj) ibn Yūsuf married her, but did not consummate the marriage. It is said that he did consummate it. Then (ʿAlī) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

Umm ʿAmr, daughter of al-Muqawwim ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, whose name was Fākhita, married (Masʿūd) ibn Muʿattib al-Thaqafī. Then (Muʿattib) ibn Abī Lahab married her. Then (Abū Sufyān) ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

Umm Kulthūm, daughter of al-Faḍl ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, married (al-Ḥasan) ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, who divorced her. Then (Abū Mūsā) al-Ashʿarī married her, then (ʿImrān) ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿUthmān al-Taymī.

Umm Jamīl, daughter of ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Qudāma ibn Ibrāhīm al-Akbar ibn Muḥammad ibn Ḥāṭib al-Jumaḥī, married (Muḥammad) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ismāʿīl ibn al-Walīd ibn Hishām ibn Ismāʿīl ibn Hishām ibn al-Walīd ibn al-Walīd ibn al-Mughīra, who divorced her. Then (ʿAlī) ibn ʿAwn ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib married her, then divorced her. Then (Ibrāhīm) ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb married her, then divorced her. Then she married (ʿAbd al-Malik) ibn Yaḥyā ibn ʿUrwa ibn al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām. Then (So-and-so ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz) ibn ʿUmar ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAnbasa ibn Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ. Then (So-and-so ibn Riyāḥ) ibn Ḥafṣ ibn ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb.

Lubāba, daughter of ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, married (ʿAlī) ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib. Then (Ismāʿīl) ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh married her. Then (Muḥammad) ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

Rabīḥa, daughter of Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar, married (Yazīd) ibn al-Walīd ibn Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān. Then (Bakkār) ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān married her, but ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAlī killed him. Then (Ṣāliḥ) ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās. Then (Isḥāq) ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Ḥasan ibn Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib.

Kayyisa, daughter of al-Ḥārith ibn Kurayz ibn Rabīʿa ibn Ḥabīb ibn ʿAbd Shams, married (Jabala) ibn Thawr ibn Humayān al-Ḥanafī. Then (Musaylima) the Liar.

Then (ʿAbd Allāh) ibn ʿĀmir ibn Kurayz, and she bore him children.

Lubāba, daughter of Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar, married (Ibrāhīm) the Imām ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās. Then (Dāwūd) ibn ʿAlī ibn …

ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās. Then Ismāʿīl ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās.

Maymūna daughter of ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib married ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, who was killed with Muṣʿab ibn al-Zubayr. Then Abū Saʿīd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām al-Makhzūmī married her. Then Nāfiʿ ibn Jubayr ibn Muṭʿim ibn ʿAdī ibn Nawfal ibn ʿAbd Manāf. Then Abū al-Sanābil ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Kurayz.

Umm ʿAmr daughter of Muḥammad ibn ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām married ʿAbd al-Malik ibn al-Ḥajjāj and bore him al-Ḥajjāj. Then Muʿāwiya ibn Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. Then ʿUmar ibn Ḥafṣ ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām.

Lubāba daughter of ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib married al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib. Then al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān married her. Then Zayd ibn al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib.

Umm Muḥammad daughter of ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās married ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās. Then ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥumayd ibn Zuhayr ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Asad ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā married her. Then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Maʿbad ibn al-ʿAbbās.

Umm Abīhā daughter of ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib married al-Ḥārith ibn al-Zubayr ibn al-Ḥārith ibn al-ʿAbbās. Then Muḥammad ibn Ṣafwān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ṣafwān ibn Umayya ibn Khalaf al-Jumaḥī married her.

Then Ibrāhīm ibn Aflaḥ ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Sufyān ibn Ḥuwayṭib ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā.

Umm Isḥāq daughter of Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh married al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib and bore him Ṭalḥa ibn al-Ḥasan. Then al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī married her. Then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq.

It is said that before ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad, Tamām ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib married her.

ʿĀʾisha daughter of Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh married ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq. Then Muṣʿab ibn al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām.

Then ʿUmar ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Maʿmar ibn ʿUthmān al-Taymī.

Umm ʿAbd Allāh daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Zayd ibn al-Khaṭṭāb ibn Nufayl married ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq.

Then ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb. Then Ṭalḥa ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq.

Asmāʾ bint ʿUmays al-Khathʿamiyya married Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib.

Then Abū Bakr al-Ṣiddīq. Then ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib.

Hind daughter of Asmāʾ ibn Khārija al-Fazārī married ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād ibn Abīhi. Then Bishr ibn Marwān ibn al-Ḥakam. Then al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf al-Thaqafī.

ʿĀtika daughter of al-Furāt ibn Muʿāwiya al-Bakkāʾī married Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab. Then ʿUmar ibn Yazīd ibn ʿUmayr al-Asadī. Then al-Ḥasan ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf al-Zuhrī. Then al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Nawfal ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. Because of his great stature, he was called “the Wave.” ʿĀtika daughter of al-Furāt ibn Muʿāwiya had seen in a dream that she broke three banners upon her breast. Her mother, al-Malāʾa daughter of Zarāra ibn Awfā al-Ḥarashiyya, went to Ibn Sīrīn and told him her dream. He said, “If her dream is true, she will marry three noble princes, all of whom will be killed while she is still their wife.” She married Yazīd ibn al-Muhallab, and he was killed. Then she married ʿUmayr21 ibn Yazīd ibn ʿUmayr, and he was killed while she was still his wife. Mālik ibn al-Mundhir ibn al-Jārūd killed him. Then al-Ḥasan ibn ʿUthmān al-Zuhrī, who was from among the people of Medina, married her. A quarrel arose between them. She said, “By God, you will be killed.” He said, “Why is that?” She told him the story. He said, “You are thrice divorced, irrevocably. Do you think I will be killed?”

She struck her face and cried out. Then al-Mawj married her—that is, al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿUbayd Allāh—and he was killed between al-Ḥīra and Kūfa. His clients killed him.

Al-Malāʾa daughter of Zarāra ibn Awfā al-Ḥarashiyya married al-Furāt ibn Muʿāwiya al-Bakkāʾī. Then Murra al-Kattān al-Tamīmī. Then ʿAbbād ibn al-Ḥuṣayn al-Ḥabṭī. Then al-Ḥusayn ibn Quṭba al-Ghassānī. Then Muḥammad ibn al-Muhallab.

Then Bishr ibn al-Mundhir ibn al-Jārūd al-ʿAbdī.

Rayyā daughter of Hāniʾ ibn Qabīṣa married ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Umm al-Ḥakam al-Thaqafī. Then a paternal cousin of hers named (…). Then al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. Then al-ʿUrbān. Then al-Haytham al-Nakhaʿī.

Hāla daughter of Sufayḥ al-Taghlibī married Basṭām ibn ʿAmr al-Taghlibī. Then ʿUbayd ibn Aws, a client of Muʿāwiya ibn Abī Sufyān. Then Hammām ibn Muṭarrif al-Taghlibī. Then Ḥajjār ibn Abjar al-ʿAjalī. Then Yūsuf ibn Hāniʾ ibn Qabīṣa al-Shaybānī. Then Sufayḥā Abū Marwān ibn Sufayḥ al-Taghlibī. Then she died while with a paternal cousin of hers from Taghlib.

Suḥayqa daughter of Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Nawfal ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib married al-Ḥārith ibn al-Muṭṭalib, from Banū Rabīʿa ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. Then ʿUmar ibn ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib. Then Ismāʿīl ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh. Then Ismāʿīl ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar, and he died. Then Ismāʿīl ibn Ibrāhīm took her back. Then ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb married her.

Umm Salama daughter of Yaʿqūb ibn Salama al-Makhzūmiyya married ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Malik, or [perhaps] Muslima. Then Abū al-ʿAbbās, the Commander of the Faithful, ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī. Then Ismāʿīl ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbbās.

Al-Kāmila daughter of Ziyād ibn ʿAthʿath al-Kalbiyya married ʿUtba ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya ibn Ṣakhr. Then Yazīd ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya. Then ʿUthmān ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya.

Āmina daughter of Saʿīd ibn al-ʿĀṣ ibn Abī Uḥayḥa married Khālid ibn Yazīd ibn Muʿāwiya. Then So-and-so ibn So-and-so; his name was not preserved. Then al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. Then Khālid ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Asīd.

Maymūna daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq married ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. Then Muḥammad ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

Then Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

Umm ʿUthmān daughter of Saʿīd ibn Khālid ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān married Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. Then al-Ḥakam ibn al-Walīd ibn Yazīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

Then Bakkār ibn Salama, from Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy. Then Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān.

Umm Kulthūm daughter of Abū Muʿayṭ married Zayd ibn Ḥāritha al-Kalbī, the client of the Messenger of God ﷺ. Then ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf al-Zuhrī.

Then al-Zubayr ibn al-ʿAwwām. Then ʿAmr ibn al-ʿĀṣ al-Sahmī.

Umm ʿUthmān daughter of Mirdās ibn Ḥuṣayn ibn ʿIdhār al-Kalbī married ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk ibn Qays al-Fihrī. Then ʿAnbasa ibn ʿAbd al-Malik ibn Marwān. Then Yaʿqūb ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Ḍaḥḥāk.

Burayka daughter of al-Qāsim ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib married ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Sahl, from Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy.

Then ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn Salama ibn ʿUmar ibn Abī Salama al-Makhzūmī. Then ʿAlī ibn al-Rabīʿ ibn ʿUbayd Allāh al-Ḥārithī. Then ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn ibn Abī Ṭālib.

Umm Bishr daughter of Abū Masʿūd—namely, ʿUqba ibn ʿAmr ibn Thaʿlaba al-Anṣārī—married Saʿīd ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAmr ibn Nufayl. Then ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Rabīʿa al-Makhzūmī. Then al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib.

Āmina daughter of Abū Sufyān ibn Ḥarb ibn Umayya married Ḥuwayṭib ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn Abī Qays, one of Banū ʿĀmir ibn Luʾayy. Then Ṣafwān ibn Umayya ibn Khalaf al-Jumaḥī. Then al-Mughīra ibn Shuʿba al-Thaqafī.

Arwā daughter of Abū Muʿayṭ ibn Abī ʿAmr married ʿĀmir ibn Ḥudhaym ibn Salāmān ibn Rabīʿa al-Jumaḥī. Then Ṭulayq ibn Sufyān ibn Umayya ibn ʿAbd Shams. Then ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād ibn Abīhi.

Umm ʿAbd Allāh daughter of ʿIyāḍ ibn ʿAdī ibn al-Khayyār ibn ʿAdī ibn Nawfal married Khālid ibn al-Akhnas al-Sahmī. Then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Muḥammad ibn Rabīʿa ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. Then al-Sarī ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Mughīra ibn al-Akhnas ibn Shurayq al-Thaqafī.

Umm Salama daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Sahl ibn ʿAmr married al-Ḥajjāj ibn Yūsuf. Then al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. Al-Walīd had written to al-Ḥajjāj, “Describe women to me.” He wrote back, “Women are cleft in two; Umm Salama daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān is pierced through.” He asked al-Ḥajjāj to give her up.

He did so. Al-Walīd married her. Then Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. Then Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

Ḥafṣa daughter of ʿImrān ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh married al-Qāsim ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUthmān ibn ʿAffān. Then Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. Then Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr ibn ʿUthmān. Then ʿAwn ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib. Then ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥasan ibn Ḥusayn. Then ʿUthmān ibn ʿUrwa ibn al-Zubayr.

Ḥafṣa daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq married al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib. Then ʿĀṣim ibn ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb.

Then al-Mundhir ibn al-Zubayr.

Maymūna daughter of ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr al-Ṣiddīq married ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿAbd al-Malik and bore him ʿAbd al-Malik and ʿAtīq. The poet said:

Generosity has departed, save the generosity of ʿAtīq,

son of ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz, born of Maymūna,

daughter of a noble, accomplished Qurashī,

whom God has decreed shall never be base-born.

Then Muḥammad ibn al-Walīd ibn Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. It is said that Sulaymān did not marry her.

Then Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

Umm Ḥakīm daughter of Yaḥyā ibn al-Ḥakam married ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz ibn al-Walīd. Then Sulaymān ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. Then Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

She was lame.

Ramla daughter of Muḥammad ibn Jaʿfar ibn Abī Ṭālib married Sulaymān ibn Hishām ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. Then Abū al-Qāsim ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿUtba ibn Abī Sufyān. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAlī killed him. Then ʿIsmāʿīl or Ṣāliḥ, the two sons of ʿAlī, married her.

Qarība daughter of Abī Umayya married ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb. She returned to the unbelievers. Then she accepted Islam, and Muʿāwiya married her. Abū Sufyān said to him:

“Are you marrying ʿUmar’s woman? Get down from his burden.”

So he divorced her. ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Bakr married her, and she bore him ʿAbd Allāh. Thus ʿĀʾisha was his paternal aunt, and Umm Salama his maternal aunt.

A daughter of Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Ḥasan ibn al-Ḥasan married Muḥammad ibn Abī al-ʿAbbās, the Commander of the Faithful. He consummated the marriage with her in Medina after her father had been killed.

When morning came, he divorced her. ʿĪsā ibn ʿAlī married her. Then Muḥammad ibn Ibrāhīm al-Imām. Then al-Ḥasan ibn Ibrāhīm ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Ḥasan. They said, however, that al-Ḥasan ibn Ibrāhīm married her sister.

Hind daughter of Suhayl ibn ʿAmr married ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAttāb ibn Asīd ibn Abī al-ʿAyyṣ. Then ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿĀmir ibn Kurayz. Then al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib.

Al-Marzubāna, who was Umm Balj, daughter of Qudayd ibn Manīʿ al-Minqarī, married Naṣr ibn Sayyār al-Laythī. Then Abū Muslim, the founder of the dynasty. Then ʿAbd al-Jabbār ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān al-Azdī, governor of Khurāsān.

ʿAbda daughter of ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥusayn ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib married ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥasan ibn ʿAlī ibn al-Ḥasan. Then Muḥammad ibn Muʿāwiya ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Jaʿfar. Then Nūḥ ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥa.

Rayṭa daughter of al-Bayyāʿ—namely, ʿAbd Shams ibn ʿAbd Yālīl ibn Nāshib ibn Ghīra ibn Saʿd ibn Layth ibn Bakr—married al-ʿĀṣ ibn Umayya. Then Ghaziya ibn Ḥudhaym ibn Saʿd ibn Sahm. Then Muṭʿim ibn ʿAdī ibn Nawfal.

Umm Qattāl daughter of ʿAbd ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Zuhra married ʿAmr ibn Umayya ibn ʿAbd Shams. Then Ḥarb ibn Umayya. Then Usayd ibn Jāriya al-Thaqafī.

Durra daughter of Abū Lahab married al-Ḥārith ibn ʿĀmir ibn Nawfal ibn…

ʿAbd Manāf. Then ʿAbbād ibn Jābir ibn Shaybān al-Sulamī. Then al-Ḥabb Usāma ibn Zayd.

Al-Nābigha bt. Ḥarmala, of ʿAnaza, married ʿAfīf ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ. Then Abū Umāma ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā, of Banū Mālik ibn Ḥisl.

Then Athātha ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn Ḥarthān ibn ʿAwf ibn [ʿUbayd ibn] ʿUwayj ibn ʿAdī ibn Kaʿb. Then al-ʿĀṣ ibn Wāʾil al-Sahmī.

Ṣafiyya bt. [Rabīʿa] ibn ʿAbd Shams married ʿUthmān ibn al-Sharīd ibn Suwayd ibn Hurmī ibn ʿĀmir ibn Makhzūm. Then Abū al-ʿĀṣ ibn Umayya. Then Azhar ibn ʿAmr ibn Naḍla ibn Ghubshān al-Khuzāʿī.

Hāla bt. Khuwaylid ibn Asad married Wahb ibn ʿUbayd [possibly ʿAbd] ibn Jābir ibn ʿAbbād [possibly ʿUbayd] ibn Mālik al-Thaqafī.22 Then al-Rabīʿ ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā ibn ʿAbd Shams. Then his brother Rabīʿa ibn ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā. Then Qaṭan ibn Wahb ibn ʿAmr ibn Ḥabīb al-Muṣṭaliqī.

Umm Yaḥyā bt. Muḥammad ibn ʿUrwa ibn al-Zubayr married al-Ḥakam ibn Yaḥyā ibn ʿUrwa ibn al-Zubayr. Then Umayya ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Usayd. Then al-Ḥakam ibn Yaḥyā ibn al-Ḥakam ibn Abī al-ʿĀṣ. Then Muḥammad ibn ʿImrān ibn Ṭalḥa ibn ʿUbayd Allāh. Then al-Ḥakam ibn Yaḥyā ibn ʿUrwa took her back, and then divorced her.

Umm Farwa bt. Abī Quḥāfa married Tamīm al-Dārī, who divorced her. Then Abū Umāma ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Bajalī. Then Umaym ibn al-Ḥārith al-Azdī, of Banū al-Ṣaqʿab; she bore him a daughter. Then al-Ashʿath ibn Qays; she bore him Muḥammad, Isḥāq, and Ismāʿīl. He said: Tamīm al-Dārī was the first of Umm Farwa’s husbands. He divorced her, and Abū Umāma ibn ʿAbd Allāh al-Bajalī married her. Then Umaym ibn al-Ḥārith al-Azdī. Then al-Ashʿath.

Khadīja bt. Khuwaylid ﵂ married Abū Hāla, Hind ibn al-Nabbāsh al-Asadī. She bore him Hind ibn Abī Hāla. Then ʿAtīq ibn ʿĀʾidh ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn Makhzūm married her after him. She bore him a daughter called Hind. Then the Messenger of God ﷺ married her after him.

Umm Sufyān bt. Ibrāhīm ibn Ibrāhīm ibn ʿAbd al-Aswad ibn Mālik ibn Ḥisl married ʿAtīq ibn ʿAlī ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn al-Zubayr ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib.

Then al-Aswad ibn al-Aswad ibn Hāshim ibn Abī Kabīr ibn ʿAbd ibn Quṣayy. Then ʿAbd al-Malik ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī Rabīʿa al-Makhzūmī.

Umm Ḥakīm bt. Khālid ibn Qāriẓ married ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn ʿAwf al-Zuhrī. Then ʿUbayd Allāh ibn al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. Then Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqāṣ.

Al-Raʿūm bt. Iyās ibn Shuʿba ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Hāniʾ ibn Qubayṣa al-Shaybānī married ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād ibn Ẓabyān, one of Banū Taym Allāt ibn Thaʿlaba ibn ʿUkāba. Then ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn al-Mundhir ibn al-Jārūd al-ʿAbdī.

Then Qutayba ibn Muslim ibn ʿAmr al-Bāhilī. Then Muḥammad ibn al-Muhallab ibn Abī Ṣufra.

Gharāʾ bt. Ḍirār ibn al-Qaʿqāʿ ibn Maʿbad ibn Zurāra married Muslim ibn ʿAmr al-Bāhilī. Then ʿAbbād ibn al-Ḥuṣayn al-Ḥabṭī. Then Muqātil ibn Mismāʿ, one of Banū Qays ibn Thaʿlaba.

ʿUmayra bt. Qays ibn Suwayd al-Bakrī, also said to be al-Khawlānī, married Saʿd ibn Abī Saʿd ibn Abī Ṭalḥa, of Banū ʿAbd al-Dār. She bore him ʿAbd Allāh.

Al-Aswad ibn Abī al-Bakhtarī married her after him, and she bore him Hind. Then Abū Ḥabīb ibn Umayya ibn Abī Ḥudhayfa ibn al-Mughīra; she bore him ʿĀtika. Then Abū Muslim ibn al-Ḥārith ibn ʿĀmir ibn Nawfal; she bore him no children. Then Faḍāla ibn Jaʿfar ibn Rifāʿa al-Makhzūmī married her after him; she bore him no children. Then Rāfiʿ al-Zubaydī, the confederate of al-Ḥārith ibn Hishām; she bore him Rifāʿa. She had been with Rāfiʿ previously.

ʿUmayra had been taken captive by Abū al-Mundhir ibn Abī Umayya ibn ʿĀbid ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn Makhzūm.

ʿĀtika bt. ʿĀmir ibn Shaddād ibn Rakāna married Yanfaʿ ibn ʿAbd, of Dhū Ruʿayn. Then Ṭalḥa ibn Muḥammad ibn Ṭalḥa. Then al-Yasaʿ ibn al-Mughīra ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān. Then Azhar ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Azhar.

Umm Ḥasan bt. al-Ḥakam ibn ʿAbd Allāh married Makhrama ibn ʿAbd al-Malik. She bore him Rifāʿa. Then ʿAbd al-Malik ibn al-Qāsim ibn ʿAbd al-Malik.

She bore him al-Qāsim, Muḥammad, and daughters. Then Muṣʿab ibn Muḥammad ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Abī ʿUmra. She bore him Umm ʿAbd Allāh and al-Qāsim.

Umm Kulthūm bt. ʿUjayr ibn ʿAbd Yazīd married Muḥammad ibn ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭālib, but bore him no children. Then Yaʿqūb ibn al-Muṭṭalib ibn ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ḥāritha ibn Yazīd ibn Shuraḥbīl ibn Yazīd ibn al-Sakūn; she bore him Ḥafṣa. Then Jaʿfar ibn Abī Sufyān ibn Usayd ibn Jāriya. She bore him ʿAbd Allāh, ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, and ʿĀtika. Then Ḥusayn ibn Abī Sufyān ibn Usayd. She bore him Yūnus.

Umm Ayyūb bt. ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmāra ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿUqba married Umayya ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Khālid ibn Usayd. He divorced her. Then ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Ziyād married her after him, and she bore him children. Then Abān ibn al-Walīd ibn ʿUqba married her after him.

Bishr ibn Marwān married Umm Ḥakīm bt. Muḥammad ibn ʿUmāra ibn ʿUqba. She bore him ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz. Then ʿAmr ibn Marwān married her after him. Then ʿUbayd Allāh ibn Marwān.

Women who bore distinguished sons

The Arabs did not consider a woman to have borne distinguished sons unless she had at least three noble sons.

The first of them was Fāṭima bt. ʿAmr ibn ʿĀʾidh ibn ʿImrān ibn Makhzūm. She bore al-Zubayr, Abū Ṭālib—both arbiters of Quraysh—and ʿAbd Allāh, the father of the Messenger of God ﷺ.

Lubāba bt. al-Ḥārith ibn Ḥazn ibn Buḥayr ibn al-Hazm al-Hilāliyya bore al-Faḍl, the Rearguard; ʿAbd Allāh, the Scholar; ʿUbayd Allāh, the Generous; Maʿbad, martyred in Ifrīqiya; ʿAbd al-Raḥmān, martyred in Ifrīqiya; and Qutham, martyred at Samarkand—the sons of al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib. Al-Faḍl died in Syria during the plague of ʿAmwās; ʿAbd Allāh died at al-Ṭāʾif, and ʿUbayd Allāh in Medina.

ʿĀtika bt. Murra ibn Fālij ibn Dhakwān al-Sulamiyya bore Hāshim, ʿAbd Shams, and al-Muṭṭalib, the sons of ʿAbd Manāf. In their age they had no equals among the Arabs.

Hind bt. Sanna ibn Sinān ibn Jāriya ibn ʿAbd al-Sulamiyya bore Yazīd, the Man of the Two Lances; Huraym; Surāqa; Anas; Hubayra; and ʿAbbās, the sons of Mirdās ibn Abī ʿĀmir al-Sulamī.

Fāṭima bt. Saʿd ibn Sayal—that is, Khayr ibn Ḥamāla, of al-Azd—bore Quṣayy, the undisputed lord of Muḍar, and Zuhra, the sons of Kilāb;

and Razāḥ and Ḥunn, the sons of Rabīʿa ibn Ḥarām ibn Ḍanna ibn ʿAbd ibn Kabīr ibn ʿUdhrā ibn Saʿd. They were both lords of Quḍāʿa, and Quḍāʿa united behind Razāḥ ibn Rabīʿa.

Salmā bt. ʿAmr ibn Zayd ibn Lubayd al-Najjāriyya bore ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib, the lord of Muḍar in his time, and so she bore distinguished sons. From Uḥayḥa ibn al-Jallāḥ ibn al-Ḥuraysh ibn Jaḥjabā al-Awsī she had ʿAmr and Maʿbad. Thus her distinction lay in ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib rather than in her two other sons. Had ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib been like them, she would not have been counted among the women who bore distinguished sons.

Ḥubbayy bt. Ḥulayl ibn Ḥabashiyya ibn Salūl al-Khuzāʿiyya bore ʿAbd al-Dār, ʿAbd Manāf, ʿAbd al-ʿUzzā, and ʿAbd, the sons of Quṣayy ibn Kilāb.

The noble, dutiful one among them was ʿAbd Manāf. Through him she bore distinguished sons, unlike his brothers; he had no equal among them.

Fāṭima bt. Asad ibn Hāshim ibn ʿAbd Manāf bore Ṭālib, ʿAqīl, Jaʿfar, and ʿAlī, the sons of Abū Ṭālib. Through Jaʿfar and ʿAlī she bore distinguished sons, unlike their two brothers.

Rayṭa bt. Saʿīd ibn Sahm ibn ʿAmr ibn Huṣayṣ bore Hishām, whose death Quraysh made their dating point; Abū Ḥudhayfa, who was Mahsham; Abū Rabīʿa, namely ʿAmr, the Man of the Two Lances, who fought on the day of al-Fijār with two lances; Abū Umayya, namely Ḥudhayfa, the Provider for the Caravan; Abū Zuhayr, namely Tamīm; and Abū ʿAbd Manāf, namely al-Fākih—the sons of al-Mughīra ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿUmar ibn Makhzūm. Of them, Ibn al-Zibaʿrī says:

/ By God, what has the sisterof Banū Sahm given birth to?

Hishām and Abū ʿAbdManāf, champion of the foe,

and the Man of the Two Lances,lion cubs among the people,men of resolve.

These two drive back the foe,while this one shoots from nearby.

No brothers betweenthe palaces of Syria and the rampart

are more virtuous than Rayṭa’s sons,or more balanced in judgment.

Māwiyya bt. Muʿāwiya ibn Zayd ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Dārim bore Laqīṭ, Ḥājib, and ʿAlqama, the sons of Zurāra ibn ʿAdas ibn Zayd ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Dārim. Laqīṭ led all of Ḥanẓala on the day of Jabala. Ḥājib ransomed himself for one thousand one hundred she-camels on the day of Jabala. ʿAlqama, too, was a leader.

Fāṭima bt. al-Kharshab al-Anmāriyya bore the complete men of Banū ʿAbs: al-Rabīʿ, the Complete; Qays, the Protector; ʿUmāra, the Bounteous; and Anas, the Horsemen’s Champion, the sons of Ziyād ibn Sufyān ibn ʿAbd Allāh ibn Nāshib ibn Hadm ibn ʿAwdh ibn Ghālib.

Khubayya bt. Riyāḥ ibn Rabīʿa al-Ghanawiyya bore Khālid, the Redhead; Mālik, the One with the Broken Nose; and Rabīʿa, al-Aḥwaṣ, the sons of Jaʿfar ibn Kilāb.

Umm al-Banīn bt. ʿAmr ibn ʿĀmir ibn Rabīʿa ibn ʿĀmir ibn Ṣaʿṣaʿa—and ʿAmr ibn ʿĀmir was “the Horseman of al-Ḍaḥyāʾ”—bore Abū Barāʾ, the Juggler of Spears; Ṭufayl, the Horseman of Qarzal; Rabīʿa, the Spring of the Poor; Muʿāwiya, the Discipliner of the Sages; and Salmā, the Champion of the Narrow Pass, the sons of Mālik ibn Jaʿfar ibn Kilāb.

ʿAfrāʾ bt. ʿUbayd ibn Thaʿlaba ibn Ghanm ibn Mālik ibn al-Najjār bore Muʿādh, Muʿawwidh, and ʿAwf, the sons of al-Ḥārith ibn Rifāʿa ibn Sawād ibn Mālik ibn Ghanm; and Khālid, Iyās, ʿĀqil, and ʿĀmir, the sons of Bukayr ibn ʿAbd Yālīl ibn Nāshib ibn Ghayra ibn Saʿd ibn Layth. They all fought at Badr with the Messenger of God ﷺ. Muʿādh, Muʿawwidh, and ʿĀqil were martyred at Badr. Khālid was martyred at al-Rajīʿ. ʿĀmir was martyred at Biʾr Maʿūna. Iyās was martyred at al-Yamāma. Of her sons, only ʿAwf remained.

Ḥamna bt. Sufyān ibn Umayya ibn ʿAbd Shams bore Saʿd, whose prayers were answered; ʿUmayr, martyred at Badr; and ʿĀmir, one of those who emigrated to Abyssinia, the sons of Mālik ibn Ahib ibn ʿAbd Manāf ibn Zuhra.

Sukhayla bt. Khuzāʿī ibn al-Ḥuwayrith ibn al-Ḥārith ibn Ḥabīb ibn Mālik ibn Ḥuṭayṭ ibn Jusham ibn Thaqīf bore ʿUbayda, al-Ḥuṣayn, and al-Ṭufayl, who fought at Badr with the Messenger of God ﷺ. ʿUbayda was martyred that day and died at al-Ṣafrāʾ. She also bore Abū Ḥudhayfa, Abū Dabāʾa, Abū Athātha, Abū al-Rabīʿ, Shimrān, Anīs, and Anas, and Khuzāʿī, Muʿāwiya, al-Mughīra, and Muʿiyya, the sons of al-Ḥārith ibn ʿAbd al-Muṭṭalib ibn ʿAbd Manāf.

Unaysa bt. al-Waḥīd ibn Kilāb bore ʿAwf, Shurayḥ, and ʿAmr, the sons of al-Aḥwaṣ ibn Jaʿfar ibn Kilāb.
